# Golden case catalog

778 cases, 24.35 MB.


## climate_flow

| case | function | KB | what it checks |

| `ascent.anticyclone.north` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.ascent` | 26 | Ascent of a northern anticyclone (radius 0.3, weight -0.45): a single gaussian of radius 1.3 r = 0.39 with a negative sign (subsidence), no comma and no eye. Float32 patch. Tolerance rtol 5e-5, atol 5e-6: over the single-vortex float32 cases the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 3.1e-7 and a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 9e-7 (the oldest low, 0.44 of the default band); tolerance 10 times the worst, rounded up. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `ascent.anticyclone.south` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.ascent` | 46 | Ascent of a southern anticyclone: the same gaussian (the kind does not depend on the hemisphere), centered at lat -33. Float64 directions, so the exp is float64 and the result float32 rounded. Tolerance rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6 (the default): the output is float32, so a float64 nudge of the inputs changes nothing (0 observed) and the other float64 order (reversed vortices, one cast at the end of an all-float64 accumulation) differs by at most 2.4e-7 (0.03 of the band). Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |

| `ascent.cyclone.equator_minus_zero` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.ascent` | 26 | A cyclone at lat = -0.0: the poleward shift is multiplied by math.copysign(1, lat), which is +1 for +0.0 and -1 for -0.0, so the comma head lands north of the center for +0.0 and south for -0.0 (a port must copy the sign bit, not test lat >= 0). Float32 patch. Tolerance rtol 5e-5, atol 5e-6: over the single-vortex float32 cases the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 3.1e-7 and a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 9e-7 (the oldest low, 0.44 of the default band); tolerance 10 times the worst, rounded up. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |

| `ascent.cyclone.pole_colat_1p00001e-9` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.ascent` | 7 | Ascent of a cyclone 1.00001e-09 rad from the north pole (lon 2.6, age 1.25): its east vector cross(z, c) has norm 1.00001e-09 (the sine of the colatitude), above the 1e-9 of the code, so the NORMAL east vector is used and the comma head shifts accordingly. Together the eight pole_colat_* cases (0.5, 0.9, 0.99, 0.99999 below; 1.00001, 1.01, 1.1, 2 above) pin the threshold 1e-9 to 1e-5 relative (the colatitude of a double latitude near pi/2 is itself quantized by 2.2e-16, i.e. 2.2e-7 relative, and the assertion that builds each case checks which side of 1e-9 the computed norm falls): a port with 1e-8, 1e-10 or 1.0001e-9 fails on at least one. 21 x 21 float32 patch of half-angle 0.5 rad. Measured on these eight patches: the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 1.2e-7 and a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 2.4e-7 (0.05 of the band). Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). Tolerance rtol 5e-5, atol 5e-6: over the single-vortex float32 cases the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 3.1e-7 and a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 9e-7 (the oldest low, 0.44 of the default band); tolerance 10 times the worst, rounded up. |

Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). Tolerance rtol 5e-5, atol 5e-6: over the single-vortex float32 cases the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 3.1e-7 and a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 9e-7 (the oldest low, 0.44 of the default band); tolerance 10 times the worst, rounded up. |

Tolerance rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6 (the default): the output is float32, so a float64 nudge of the inputs changes nothing (0 observed) and the other float64 order (reversed vortices, one cast at the end of an all-float64 accumulation) differs by at most 2.4e-7 (0.03 of the band). Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |

Tolerance rtol 2e-4, atol 2e-5: the eye gaussian has a radius of 0.0072, so the float32 subtraction p - c next to the center moves the exponent: the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 6e-6 and a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 1.8e-5 (1.96 of the default band); tolerance 10 times that. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |

Tolerance rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6 (the default): measured on this very case (a float64 patch around the storm, float32 output): a one-ulp nudge of every float64 direction (20 draws) and the squared distance recomputed in exact rational arithmetic both changed nothing after the cast to float32 (0 observed), because the eye exponent d2 / (0.12 r)^2 turns a 1e-16 error of d2 into only 3e-14; the float32-direction version of the same patch differs by 8.3e-7, which is why the float32 case has its own, looser tolerance. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `backtrace.earth.default` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.backtrace` | 9 | Back-trace (default 48 steps, time 1) of 391 float32 directions through the Earth-like flow: poles, antimeridian, every vortex center and its surroundings. Tolerance rtol 4e-5, atol 4e-6: the loop is float64, so reversing the vortex order changes nothing at float32 (0 observed); a one-ulp nudge of the float32 directions moves the output by at most 1.8e-6 over the steps and time variants (0.37 of the default band; the shear of the flow stretches the nudge a few times), tolerance 10 times that. An integration done entirely in float32 (numpy, same loop) stays inside it (max 2.2e-6), so a float32 port can be checked against this case. | `build_flow.earth.seed0` | `cloudgen.flow.build_flow` | 1 | The vortex list build_flow draws for body earth and seed 0 (23 vortices: cyclone, anticyclone pairs per hemisphere then Poisson(1.5) tropical storms), as arrays in vortex order (kind 0 cyclone, 1 anticyclone, 2 tropical; lon, lat, radius, omega, ascent, age) and the four flow scalars (hadley_deg, jet, trades, polar_easterlies). The numbers come from numpy default_rng(seed ^ 0x5F10): a port with another generator CANNOT reproduce lon, lat, radius, omega or age, so this case is tagged rng; it binds a port only through flow_scalars, the vortex count per kind and hemisphere and the sign of omega (see build_flow.structure.bodies and the stat cases) and it feeds the kernels real vortex lists. | `build_flow.stats.earth` | `cloudgen.flow.build_flow` | 1 | Distribution of the vortex population of body earth over seeds 0..299: the checks a port with another random generator must pass, because its draws cannot equal numpy's. (1) The number of cyclones and of anticyclones per hemisphere is EXACTLY m (count.*, exact). (2) Each drawn quantity, normalized to its uniform draw, lies inside its theoretical .bound (hard check) and its sample .range [min, max] over the 300 seeds comes within 10% of the bound width of that bound (the Python gaps are at most 5%, in the thin tails of the radius product and of the triangular longitude gap; a missed extreme at 10% has a probability under 1e-4): cyclone lat offset from the track over phi_H, radius over L_R, omega over (hemi 3 age), age, the longitude gap of consecutive lows over their spacing minus 1; anticyclone /lat/ over phi_H, radius over L_R, omega over (-hemi 1.2), the longitude offset from its low over half the spacing; tropical /lat/ over phi_H, radius over 0.35 L_R, omega over (hemi 12) and lon. hemi = sign(lat) times the sign of the rotation period, so the omega ranges also check the sign by hemisphere and by spin; omega_positive_fraction (kind cyclone, anticyclone, tropical; hemisphere north, south) is exact (0 or 1). (3) The number of tropical cyclones per hemisphere is Poisson(1.5): tropical.count.pooled_mean over the 2 x 300 samples has standard error sqrt(1.5 / 600) = 0.05, a port passes when its pooled mean over 300 seeds is within 4 standard errors (0.2) of 1.5 and the chi-square of its histogram against 600 tropical.count.poisson_pmf over the six bins 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 and >= 5 (expected counts 133.9, 200.8, 150.6, 75.3, 28.2, 11.1) stays below 20.5 (5 degrees of freedom, p = 0.001; the Python value is 2 to 8). Conventions the statistics depend on: the vortex longitudes are NOT wrapped to [-pi, pi] (cyclone i of a hemisphere is at lon0 + (i + jitter) 2 pi / m with lon0 in [-pi, pi], so up to about 2 pi (m - 0.7) / m + pi, and the anticyclone at the longitude of its low plus pi / m times a factor), cyclone.lon_gap uses the difference of consecutive unwrapped longitudes in creation order and anticyclone.lon_offset pairs the i-th cyclone with the i-th anticyclone of the same hemisphere in creation order (a port that wraps its longitudes must take the differences before wrapping or unwrap them); the lows and highs of one hemisphere are drawn in the order low i, high i, and the tropical storms follow after the last pair. Layout of the arrays: tropical.count.histogram has 10 bins, the pooled per-hemisphere tropical counts 0, 1, ..., 8 and >= 9; tropical.count.poisson_pmf has 10 entries, the Poisson(1.5) probabilities of 0..8 and the tail 1 - sum at index 9 (merge bins 5.. for the chi-square); tropical.count.pooled_std uses ddof = 1 over the 600 per-hemisphere counts; tropical.count.mean_by_hemisphere is (north, south); omega_positive_fraction is [kind, hemisphere] (kind order cyclone, anticyclone, tropical; north, south). Python-vs-Python tolerances: float64 rtol 1e-9. |

| `build_flow.structure.bodies` | `cloudgen.flow.build_flow` | 4 | The part of build_flow that does NOT depend on the random draws (asserted equal over seeds 0..19), per body of params.names: the four flow scalars (hadley_deg, jet, trades, polar_easterlies; weak shear 0.3 / 0.1 / 0 and hadley 90 where there is no storm track), the number of cyclones and of anticyclones per hemisphere (north, south), both exactly m = max(2, round(x)) with x = 2 pi cos(track) / (5 L_R) (output wavenumber_x, NaN for a body without a storm track; Python round is half-to-even, the floor 2 is taken by threshold_below_50), and 0 for a body without a storm track (including every no-spin row), and the constant ascent weight per kind (cyclone 1, anticyclone -0.45, tropical 1.6) and the age of the non-cyclones (1), both asserted over the seeds. The 16 wave_<k>p5_below/above bodies put x 5e-5 (relative) below and above k + 0.5 for k = 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 11, 14, 20 (m = k below, k + 1 above), so a wavelength factor (the 5 of 5 L_R) or a deformation radius off by more than 0.005 per cent changes m on one of them; no row sits exactly on a half-integer (the round-half-to-even tie is not pinned, it depends on the last bit of the libm). The input table holds NaN (a None or NaN period), +inf, 0, -0 and +-1e300 periods: no spin, no storm track. A port with any random generator must reproduce all of it exactly (scalars and wavenumber_x rtol 1e-9). Exact for the integers. NaN and infinities may appear in the inputs (a rotation period None is stored as NaN) and in the outputs: NaN equals NaN, an infinity equals the same infinity (sign included). |

Python-vs-Python tolerance: float64 rtol 1e-9, kind exact. |

| `has_baroclinic_eddies.bodies` | `cloudgen.climate.has_baroclinic_eddies` | 1 | Whether each row of the named bodies (Earth, Mars, Titan, Venus, a 0.4-day fast rotator, a retrograde Earth, a 4-day slow rotator, a 2-radius super-Earth, a small moon, five bodies at the 50 degree eddy threshold) and degenerate rows (no spin as None/NaN/inf/-inf/0/-0, subnormal and huge periods, zero, negative, NaN and infinite gravity, scale height and radius, odd regime strings) has a storm track (raw Hadley edge STRICTLY below 50 degrees): a boolean, exact. Rows within 1e-6 of the threshold sit on both sides, and threshold_exact_50 (period 1.6621166666666667 d with the Earth radius, gravity and scale height: every ratio but the spin is exactly 1, so the raw edge is exactly 50.0 in IEEE arithmetic, a correctly rounded division of a correctly rounded division) is false, which pins the strict <; its neighbors one ulp of the period away have raw edges 49.99999999999999 (true) and 50.000000000000014 (false). A NaN raw edge (NaN radius) is false, an infinite one false. NaN and infinities may appear in the inputs (a rotation period None is stored as NaN) and in the outputs: NaN equals NaN, an infinity equals the same infinity (sign included). |

NaN and infinities may appear in the inputs (a rotation period None is stored as NaN) and in the outputs: NaN equals NaN, an infinity equals the same infinity (sign included). |

| `map_width_for_texel.default_cap` | `cloudgen.climate.map_width_for_texel` | 1 | map_width_for_texel(radius_km, texel_km) with the default cap 16384 (params.max_width): the radii of the defaults case with the texel cycling through 3.0, 2.0 and 5.5 km (inputs.texel_km), so that a finer texel reaches the cap for most planets. Integers: exact. |
| `map_width_for_texel.default_texel` | `cloudgen.climate.map_width_for_texel` | 1 | map_width_for_texel(radius_km, max_width=...) with the default texel 3.0 km (params.texel_km): the radii of the defaults case with the cap alternating 4096 and 1 << 20 (inputs.max_width), so that the width is the power of two of the 3 km texel when the cap is not reached. Integers: exact. |
| `map_width_for_texel.defaults` | `cloudgen.climate.map_width_for_texel` | 0 | map_width_for_texel(radius_km) with BOTH defaults, as the callers use it: the target texel is 3.0 km (MAP_TEXEL_KM) and the cap 16384 (MAP_MAX_WIDTH), given in params and in the constants case. The radii include widths 0.5 per cent above and below 2^4, 2^7, 2^9, 2^11, 2^12 and 2^13 at 3.0 km (a default texel off by 1 per cent flips them), the planets, 7822 and 7824 km (a width just below and just above 16384) and radii far past the cap (10000, 50000, 1e6 km give the cap 16384), and 1, 0.1, 0 and -5 km (width 2). Integers: exact. |
| `map_width_for_texel.log2_window` | `cloudgen.climate.map_width_for_texel` | 0 | The one place the rule is not an exact ceil: radii (inputs.radius_km, texel 3, cap 1 << 20) whose w = 2 pi radius / texel is exactly ONE ULP above 2^5, 2^8, 2^10, 2^12 and 2^14. math.log2 returns the integer itself (the true value is 0.2 to 0.4 ulp above it and rounds back), so ceil gives k and the Python width is 2^k (32, 256, 1024, 4096, 16384), where the exact rule would give 2^(k+1) (64, 512, 2048, 8192, 32768; params.exact_rule_width, informational: NOT the expected output). The oracle follows math.log2: a port must call its libm log2 on w and take the ceil, not test w against a power of two in integers. This relies on log2 being correctly rounded here (error below 0.5 ulp, which the Python platform libm gives); a log2 with up to 1 ulp of error may return the next double and then agrees with the exact rule instead, so these rows are the behavior of the oracle, not a portable truth. Integers: exact. |
| `map_width_for_texel.table` | `cloudgen.climate.map_width_for_texel` | 1 | Map width from a target texel, all three arguments given: w = ((2 pi) radius) / texel in double (a true division, not a multiplication by the reciprocal), then 2 ** ceil(log2(max(w, 2))) (a power of two, never below 2), capped by max_width (the cap is returned as is, even when not a power of two, or 0 or 1). Rows: the planets (Earth and Venus 16384, Mars and Titan 8192, the Moon 4096), caps, tiny, zero and negative radius and a negative texel (width 2), 1e300 km, an exact power of two (1024 stays 1024), one part in 1e9 below and above 4096, and widths 1e-14 .. 1e-11 (relative) above 2^3, 2^7, 2^10, 2^12 and 2^14 (the next power of two, 16 .. 32768). Integers: exact. A port computes the ceil from a libm log2 (Python math.log2, Rust f64::log2) exactly as written; an exact integer test would agree on every row of this table and differ only in the map_width_for_texel.log2_window case. A port must compare with the cap in floating point or saturate before shifting (2 ** 996 overflows a u64). The calls that raise in Python (a NaN or infinite radius: ValueError or OverflowError from math.ceil; a zero texel: ZeroDivisionError) are not cases. |

| `velocity.earth.float64` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.velocity` | 42 | Tangent velocity of the Earth-like flow (23 vortices: lows, highs, tropical storms of both hemispheres plus the zonal shear) at 891 float64 directions: the poles, the special set and, for each vortex, its exact center (the vortex term vanishes there: cross(c, c) = 0) and two nearby points. Float64 gaussians of squared chord distance: rtol 1e-9; atol 1e-12 covers the cancellation of cross(c, p) next to a center (absolute error 1e-16 times omega up to 12). Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `velocity.non_unit_input` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.velocity` | 0 | Velocity at points that are NOT unit directions (/z/ above 1, below 1, on an axis): outside the contract, but the latitude is arcsin(clip(z, -1, 1)), so it is defined: z = 2 and z = -3 give the poles, and the vortex distance and cross use the raw (unnormalized) p. Float64: rtol 1e-9. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `velocity.still_earth` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.velocity` | 42 | The Earth-like vortices with the zonal shear switched off (jet = trades = polar_easterlies = 0), the flow products.wind_map builds: only the vortex terms remain, and the poles and zonal points with no vortex nearby are 0 to rounding. Float64: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `velocity.stress.float32` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.velocity` | 16 | The stress flow with float32 directions (pole, antimeridian and signed-zero vortices, a 0.02 and a 1.5 radius). Output float32. Tolerance rtol 1e-4, atol 1e-5: the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 3.5e-7 (0.28 of the default band), a one-ulp nudge of the directions by 1.1e-6 (0.91; the cross product next to a vortex center cancels), reversing the vortex order by 1.2e-7; tolerance 10 times the worst. Values reach 1. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `velocity.stress.float64` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.velocity` | 31 | Tangent velocity of the stress flow (hadley 21.2, weak shear 0.3 / 0.1 / 0): vortices on both poles, around the east-vector fallback, on the antimeridian at lon +pi and -pi, on the equator at lat +0.0 and -0.0, a 0.02 and a 1.5 radius, a zero-omega one, two on one center. Float64: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `velocity.zonal_only` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.velocity` | 29 | Tangent velocity of the default zonal flow (no vortices) at 622 float64 unit directions: both poles (the velocity is exactly 0 there: cross(z, p) = 0), the equator at nine longitudes with both antimeridian columns, a pair straddling it, mid-latitudes. Float64: a few products and sums of the float64 rate: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). |
| `vortex.center` | `cloudgen.flow.Vortex.center` | 1 | The unit direction of a vortex center from its (lon, lat) in radians: both poles, longitudes +pi and -pi (the antimeridian: cos is -1 and sin is +-1.2e-16), signed zeros, longitudes beyond +-pi. Float64 libm cos and sin (correctly rounded to 1 ulp): rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-14 for the 6e-17 of cos(pi/2). Conventions of cloudgen.sphere read here: lonlat_to_dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat cos lon, cos lat sin lon, sin lat) with float64 numpy cos and sin (right-handed, z north, longitude grows east; a vortex center is that direction); normalize(v) = v / /v/ (plain division, no epsilon: a zero vector gives NaN). A pseudo-function name: there is nothing to call, the port maps it by hand. (a property: the port maps it to its own center function). |
| `zonal_rate.default` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.zonal_rate` | 11 | Zonal drift profile (jet minus trades minus the polar easterlies, relative to the storm track) of the default flow (hadley 30) on 721 latitudes from -90 to +90 degrees in radians, both poles and the equator included. Float64 closed form of gaussians and a smoothstep: rtol 1e-9; the value is zero at x = 1.7 (lat 51) up to rounding, which atol 1e-12 absorbs. |
| `zonal_rate.float32_lat` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.zonal_rate` | 4 | Zonal drift profile of the default flow for a float32 latitude array: the profile is evaluated in float32 and the constant prof(1.7) subtracted in float64, so the output is float64 holding float32 rounding. Tolerance rtol 2e-5, atol 2e-6: the float64-promoted computation differs by at most 1.6e-7, a one-ulp nudge of the latitudes by 2.5e-7 (0.13 of the default band, the largest relative part next to the zero crossings), tolerance 10 times that. |
| `zonal_rate.hadley_and_shear` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.zonal_rate` | 51 | Zonal drift profile for the (hadley_deg, jet, trades, polar_easterlies) rows of params.flows, on the same latitudes: hadley 10 and 90 (the clamp bounds, where the polar smoothstep saturates early and never), all-zero shear (the profile is exactly 0), the weak-shear flow of a body with no storm track (0.3, 0.1, 0), and negative or large coefficients. Output row i is flows[i]. Float64: rtol 1e-9. |
| `zonal_rate.out_of_range_lat` | `cloudgen.flow.Flow.zonal_rate` | 0 | Zonal drift profile at latitudes beyond +-pi/2 (the arcsin of the callers never produces them, but the formula uses /lat/ and is defined), signed zeros and radians(51), where the rate is 0. Float64: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |

## metrics_match

| case | function | KB | what it checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `area_histogram.all_dropped` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 0 | Every value outside [0, 1]: the histogram is 0 / 0 = NaN in every bin (numpy warns). |
| `area_histogram.bins1` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 1 | One bin, the whole [0, 1] range: the histogram is [1.0]. Random float32 values in [0, 1). np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Float64 sums of weights: 1e-9. |
| `area_histogram.bins4` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 1 | 4 bins (the edges 0.25, 0.5, 0.75 are exact in float32). Random float32 values in [0, 1). np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Float64 sums of weights: 1e-9. |
| `area_histogram.bins7` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 1 | 7 bins (the edges k / 7 are not exact in float32: they are rounded to float32). Random float32 values in [0, 1). np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Float64 sums of weights: 1e-9. |
| `area_histogram.earth256` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 32 | Area-weighted 10-bin histogram of the 256x128 Earth reference. np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Byte-derived maps put thousands of texels exactly on the edges 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8 (51k / 255): a port must send a texel equal to a float32 edge to the UPPER bin (a one-ulp change of the input moves the L1 mass by 0.03 to 0.09). With that tie rule the result is a float64 sum of weights: 1e-9. Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `area_histogram.edges.f32` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 1 | Every float32 bin edge k / 10 exactly, one float32 ulp below and one above it, plus values outside [0, 1] (-0.1, 1.1, -1e30, 1e30), which are dropped (the histogram is renormalized by what is left). The edge value goes to the upper bin and 1.0 to the last: np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Float64 sums of weights: 1e-9. |
| `area_histogram.edges.f64` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 2 | The same edge ties on a float64 image: the edges are then float64 k * 0.1 (7 * 0.1 = 0.7000000000000001, so the literal 0.7 falls in bin 6, not 7). np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Weighted float64 sums: 1e-9. |
| `area_histogram.generated.iso256` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 32 | The same histogram on an isotropic generated map (256x128, seed 42, its 8-bit preview): texels at exactly 0 and 1 fill the first bin and the closed last bin. np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Float64 sum of weights: 1e-9. Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `area_histogram.nan_dropped` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 0 | Three NaN texels among random float32 values: NaN fails both range comparisons, so np.histogram drops it like any out-of-range value and the histogram is renormalized by the weights that are left. np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum. Float64 sums of weights: 1e-9. |
| `area_histogram.saturated.ones` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 1 | A fully saturated map (all 1.0): everything is in the closed last bin. np.histogram bins [edge_k, edge_k+1), the last bin closed, edges = linspace(0, 1, bins + 1) computed in float64 (k * (1 / bins)) and rounded to the IMAGE dtype (float32 edges for a float32 image); values outside [0, 1] are dropped; weights cos(lat) in float64, normalized by their sum.  |
| `area_histogram.saturated.zeros` | `cloudgen.metrics.area_histogram` | 1 | An empty map (all 0.0): everything is in the first bin. |
| `band_layers.earth64.dyadic` | `cloudgen.match.band_layers` | 42 | Band-pass layers G(s_{k-1}) - G(s_k) with G(s_-1) the image and G = _blur (scipy.ndimage.gaussian_filter(img, sigma, mode=("nearest", "wrap")): truncate 4.0 (radius int(4 sigma + 0.5), taps exp(-x^2 / (2 sigma^2)) normalized to sum 1, computed in float64), axis 0 (rows: "nearest" = clamp at the poles) THEN axis 1 (columns: "wrap" = periodic, folded as many times as the radius needs, also when it exceeds the width), each pass accumulating in float64 and rounding to the input dtype (so a float32 image is rounded to float32 between the two passes); sigma <= 1e-15 returns a copy, a radius that rounds to 0 (sigma < 0.125) is the identity.), and the low-pass residual G(s_last) (the image itself for no sigma); the layers and the residual sum back to the image (telescoping, up to float32 rounding). Layers are stacked in one array (shape (len(sigmas), h, w)). The 64x32 Earth reference, a dyadic ladder 1, 2, 4, 8 (the sigmas match_to_earth builds for Earth itself). Float32 chain (each blur rounds to float32 between its passes, a layer is a float32 difference): a float64-promoted result differs by <= 9e-8 in absolute value (a layer is a small difference, so its relative error is meaningless); the golden float32 tolerance.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `band_layers.empty_sigmas` | `cloudgen.match.band_layers` | 1 | Band-pass layers G(s_{k-1}) - G(s_k) with G(s_-1) the image and G = _blur (scipy.ndimage.gaussian_filter(img, sigma, mode=("nearest", "wrap")): truncate 4.0 (radius int(4 sigma + 0.5), taps exp(-x^2 / (2 sigma^2)) normalized to sum 1, computed in float64), axis 0 (rows: "nearest" = clamp at the poles) THEN axis 1 (columns: "wrap" = periodic, folded as many times as the radius needs, also when it exceeds the width), each pass accumulating in float64 and rounding to the input dtype (so a float32 image is rounded to float32 between the two passes); sigma <= 1e-15 returns a copy, a radius that rounds to 0 (sigma < 0.125) is the identity.), and the low-pass residual G(s_last) (the image itself for no sigma); the layers and the residual sum back to the image (telescoping, up to float32 rounding). Layers are stacked in one array (shape (len(sigmas), h, w)). No sigma: no layer (shape (0, h, w)) and the residual is the image itself, unchanged. |
| `band_layers.f64` | `cloudgen.match.band_layers` | 15 | Band-pass layers G(s_{k-1}) - G(s_k) with G(s_-1) the image and G = _blur (scipy.ndimage.gaussian_filter(img, sigma, mode=("nearest", "wrap")): truncate 4.0 (radius int(4 sigma + 0.5), taps exp(-x^2 / (2 sigma^2)) normalized to sum 1, computed in float64), axis 0 (rows: "nearest" = clamp at the poles) THEN axis 1 (columns: "wrap" = periodic, folded as many times as the radius needs, also when it exceeds the width), each pass accumulating in float64 and rounding to the input dtype (so a float32 image is rounded to float32 between the two passes); sigma <= 1e-15 returns a copy, a radius that rounds to 0 (sigma < 0.125) is the identity.), and the low-pass residual G(s_last) (the image itself for no sigma); the layers and the residual sum back to the image (telescoping, up to float32 rounding). | `band_layers.noise.fractional` | `cloudgen.match.band_layers` | 12 | Band-pass layers G(s_{k-1}) - G(s_k) with G(s_-1) the image and G = _blur (scipy.ndimage.gaussian_filter(img, sigma, mode=("nearest", "wrap")): truncate 4.0 (radius int(4 sigma + 0.5), taps exp(-x^2 / (2 sigma^2)) normalized to sum 1, computed in float64), axis 0 (rows: "nearest" = clamp at the poles) THEN axis 1 (columns: "wrap" = periodic, folded as many times as the radius needs, also when it exceeds the width), each pass accumulating in float64 and rounding to the input dtype (so a float32 image is rounded to float32 between the two passes); sigma <= 1e-15 returns a copy, a radius that rounds to 0 (sigma < 0.125) is the identity.), and the low-pass residual G(s_last) (the image itself for no sigma); the layers and the residual sum back to the image (telescoping, up to float32 rounding). Layers are stacked in one array (shape (len(sigmas), h, w)). White noise with fractional sigmas (a generated map narrower than Earth's: sigmas scaled by the width ratio); the first sigma 0.25 has radius 1. Float32 chain (each blur rounds to float32 between its passes, a layer is a float32 difference): a float64-promoted result differs by <= 9e-8 in absolute value (a layer is a small difference, so its relative error is meaningless); the golden float32 tolerance.  |

| `earth_lat.hadley30` | `cloudgen.match._earth_lat` | 1 | Earth-equivalent /latitude/ (degrees) per row: min(/degrees(lat)/ / hadley_deg * 30, 65) where 65 = EARTH_CAP_DEG - ZONE_DEG / 2 (past it the zones of Earth's ice-contaminated caps are replaced by the 62.5..67.5 zone). Earth: x * 30 is the latitude itself, up to the cap. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles, the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. Float64 closed form: 1e-9. |
| `earth_lat.hadley60` | `cloudgen.match._earth_lat` | 1 | Earth-equivalent /latitude/ (degrees) per row: min(/degrees(lat)/ / hadley_deg * 30, 65) where 65 = EARTH_CAP_DEG - ZONE_DEG / 2 (past it the zones of Earth's ice-contaminated caps are replaced by the 62.5..67.5 zone). A slow rotator (edge at 60 degrees): the zones cover half the globe's span, none reach the cap. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles pulled in by 1e-3 rad (/lat/ = 90 would sit exactly on a zone edge for this edge), the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. Float64 closed form: 1e-9. |
| `earth_lat.hadley8_5` | `cloudgen.match._earth_lat` | 1 | Earth-equivalent /latitude/ (degrees) per row: min(/degrees(lat)/ / hadley_deg * 30, 65) where 65 = EARTH_CAP_DEG - ZONE_DEG / 2 (past it the zones of Earth's ice-contaminated caps are replaced by the 62.5..67.5 zone). A fast rotator (edge at 8.5 degrees): all but the tropics are capped at zone 13. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles, the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. Float64 closed form: 1e-9. |
| `earth_lat.hadley90` | `cloudgen.match._earth_lat` | 1 | Earth-equivalent /latitude/ (degrees) per row: min(/degrees(lat)/ / hadley_deg * 30, 65) where 65 = EARTH_CAP_DEG - ZONE_DEG / 2 (past it the zones of Earth's ice-contaminated caps are replaced by the 62.5..67.5 zone). Edge at 90 degrees (no storm track): zones 0..5 only. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles pulled in by 1e-3 rad (/lat/ = 90 would sit exactly on a zone edge for this edge), the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. Float64 closed form: 1e-9. |
| `global_mean.earth256` | `cloudgen.metrics.global_mean` | 32 | Area-weighted mean: row means (float32 pairwise accumulation for a float32 image) weighted by cos(lat) in float64. A float64 accumulation of the same image differs by 2.3e-8 relative, so a port is within the float32 default. The 256x128 Earth reference. Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `global_mean.f64` | `cloudgen.metrics.global_mean` | 3 | Float64 image: every sum is float64, 1e-9. |
| `global_mean.generated.iso256` | `cloudgen.metrics.global_mean` | 32 | Area-weighted mean: row means (float32 pairwise accumulation for a float32 image) weighted by cos(lat) in float64. | `global_mean.single_texel` | `cloudgen.metrics.global_mean` | 0 | The smallest image (1x1, lat 0, weight 1): the value itself. | `judge_surface.exact_ties` | `cloudgen.metrics.judge_surface` | 0 | judge_surface(ref, maps): one row per judged metric (SURFACE_JUDGED order, params.judged; inputs hold the columns params.keys: the six judged ones then the two season terms). v = the metric over the maps; spread = sample std (ddof 1; NaN for one map); season = /ref[season term]/ or 0; tolerance = max(2.2 * spread, floor) + season with floor 2.5 for itcz_lat and 0.01 otherwise; diff = mean(v) - ref; pass = tolerance finite and /diff/ <= tolerance. Python max(nan, floor) is NaN (the first argument wins when a comparison fails): do not port it as floor. Float64 arithmetic on a handful of numbers: 1e-9; no verdict sits within 1e-6 of a tie, except in exact_ties and just_over_ties, which sit ON one (or an ulp past it) by construction, from exactly representable operations. Two IDENTICAL maps (spread exactly 0; the mean of two equal floats is exact), so tolerance = floor + season allowance with no spread term, and a reference of 0 (8.0 for itcz_lat) so that diff is the map value itself, in exact float64 arithmetic: land_minus_ocean 0.01 against the floor 0.01; subtropics_land_minus_ocean -0.01 (the negative side: abs); north_minus_south 0.01 + 0.5 against 0.01 + /-0.5/ (the season term of ocean_north_minus_south); midlat_north_minus_south -(0.01 + 0.25) against 0.01 + 0.25; itcz_lat 10.5 - 8.0 = 2.5 against its floor 2.5 (dyadic, exact); deck_minus_open -0.01 against 0.01. Every /diff/ EQUALS its tolerance bit for bit: the verdict is `<=`, so all six pass (an exclusive comparison fails all six). Every operation is a single IEEE addition, subtraction, abs or an exact mean, so the tie is portable. |
| `judge_surface.four_maps.mixed` | `cloudgen.metrics.judge_surface` | 1 | judge_surface(ref, maps): one row per judged metric (SURFACE_JUDGED order, params.judged; inputs hold the columns params.keys: the six judged ones then the two season terms). v = the metric over the maps; spread = sample std (ddof 1; NaN for one map); season = /ref[season term]/ or 0; tolerance = max(2.2 * spread, floor) + season with floor 2.5 for itcz_lat and 0.01 otherwise; diff = mean(v) - ref; pass = tolerance finite and /diff/ <= tolerance. Python max(nan, floor) is NaN (the first argument wins when a comparison fails): do not port it as floor. Float64 arithmetic on a handful of numbers: 1e-9; no verdict sits within 1e-6 of a tie, except in exact_ties and just_over_ties, which sit ON one (or an ulp past it) by construction, from exactly representable operations. Four maps over a handmade reference: land_minus_ocean passes inside 2.2 sigma, subtropics_land_minus_ocean misses by far, itcz_lat uses its 2.5 floor, north_minus_south and midlat_north_minus_south add a season allowance of 0.10 and 0.05. |

| `judge_surface.identical_maps.floor` | `cloudgen.metrics.judge_surface` | 1 | judge_surface(ref, maps): one row per judged metric (SURFACE_JUDGED order, params.judged; inputs hold the columns params.keys: the six judged ones then the two season terms). v = the metric over the maps; spread = sample std (ddof 1; NaN for one map); season = /ref[season term]/ or 0; tolerance = max(2.2 * spread, floor) + season with floor 2.5 for itcz_lat and 0.01 otherwise; diff = mean(v) - ref; pass = tolerance finite and /diff/ <= tolerance. Three identical maps: spread 0, so the tolerance is the floor (0.01, 2.5 for itcz_lat) plus the season allowance (0.10, 0.05): land_minus_ocean (diff 0) passes, subtropics_land_minus_ocean (diff 0.23 against 0.01) misses, the two season-widened metrics (diff 0.01) and itcz_lat (diff 1.0 against 2.5) pass. |

| `match_by_zone.f64_gen` | `cloudgen.match._match_by_zone` | 41 | Quantile-map each row of `gen` onto Earth's distribution at its zone, interpolated linearly between zone centers: per zone present in both gen (zone of each gen row from _zone_index(lat_g, hadley)) and `ze`, a source table src = np.quantile(gen rows of the zone, QUANTILES) + k * 1e-9 and a destination table dst = np.quantile(earth rows with ze == z, QUANTILES) (linear method: virtual index (n - 1) q, lerp between the two neighbours, float64 even for float32 data; the float64 `+ arange * 1e-9` makes src strictly increasing); per row the two nearest zone centers (z + 0.5) * 5 around the row's Earth-equivalent latitude (np.searchsorted side left, minus 1, clipped), weight t = clip((el - c_i) / (c_j - c_i), 0, 1) (0 when only one zone is left or i == j), a = np.interp(row, src_i, dst_i) (clamps to dst[0] / dst[-1] outside the table, returns dst[j] exactly at a node, slope * (x - xp[j]) + fp[j] otherwise), b likewise for zone j (skipped when t == 0), out = a + t (b - a). The result has the dtype of `gen`. Tolerance: a port-style implementation (own sort-based quantile with the float64 lerp, an interp written as slope * (x - xp) + fp and as a lerp, float32 and float64 neighbour differences) differs from numpy by at most 6e-8 on the stored inputs (the float32 rounding of the stored output): the golden float32 tolerance. The function is NOT stable against its INPUTS: a one-ulp change of a gen texel that equals a plateau value of its zone (maps from bytes have many) moves that texel by up to 2.5e-4 through the 1e-9 ladder; hand it the exact stored inputs. Gen: the isotropic generated map at 64x32 (float32); earth: Earth at 128x64 (stored u8, / 255 in float32); ze as match_to_earth builds it (zones of the Earth rows at hadley 30, -1 beyond 67.5 degrees). A float64 gen: the output is float64 (np.empty_like(gen)), the tables are the same float64; a port-style float64 implementation differs by 3.3e-10 (slopes up to 1e6 amplify the 1e-16 rounding of the tables), so 1e-8 / 5e-9.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) earth are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

| `match_to_earth.cov_f64` | `cloudgen.match.match_to_earth` | 18 | match_to_earth(cov, earth, hadley_deg, levels, strength): sigmas 2^k (k < levels) in Earth texels, scaled by the width ratio for the generated map; band_layers of both (float32), each generated layer quantile-matched per latitude zone to the same Earth layer (_match_by_zone, Earth zones from lat at hadley 30 with rows past 67.5 degrees excluded), summed with strength * matched + (1 - strength) * original, plus the matched residual, clipped to [0, 1], then matched once more as a whole to Earth's own map, and blended again by strength; the result is float32. Cov: the isotropic generated map at 64x32 (8-bit preview), Earth 128x64 (stored u8 / 255), width ratio 0.5. The cov handed to the function is float64 (the u8 map divided by 255 then cast): it is cast to float32 first (cov.astype(np.float32)); the output is float32 all the same. Input convention: the u8 input(s) cov, earth are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

Tolerance, measured on 9 runs of the case (8 with cov and Earth moved by -1, 0 or +1 float32 ulp per texel, one with every float32 intermediate promoted to float64, i.e. what a float64 port computes): the layers are float32 band-pass differences and the quantile tables turn tied values (a texel equal to a plateau value is mapped through the 1e-9 ladder) into steep steps, so a few texels move by up to 9e-4 (on average 1e-7) while the 8x8 block means move by <= 1.6e-5. Hence `out`: 1e-5 + 1e-5 /want/ for 97% of the texels (the largest fraction outside was 0.33%, frac = 10x = 0.03), block_mean8: 1e-4 + 2e-4 /want/. Cov: the isotropic generated map at 64x32 (8-bit preview), Earth 128x64 (stored u8 / 255), width ratio 0.5. One level (sigma 1 Earth texel). Input convention: the u8 input(s) cov, earth are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

| `match_to_earth.h30.strength0` | `cloudgen.match.match_to_earth` | 18 | match_to_earth(cov, earth, hadley_deg, levels, strength): sigmas 2^k (k < levels) in Earth texels, scaled by the width ratio for the generated map; band_layers of both (float32), each generated layer quantile-matched per latitude zone to the same Earth layer (_match_by_zone, Earth zones from lat at hadley 30 with rows past 67.5 degrees excluded), summed with strength * matched + (1 - strength) * original, plus the matched residual, clipped to [0, 1], then matched once more as a whole to Earth's own map, and blended again by strength; the result is float32. Extra output block_mean8 = float64 means of the 8x8 blocks of `out`, computed by the case: a robust summary that bounds the texels `out` lets differ. Tolerance: no blurred layer feeds the quantile tables here, so the f32 pipeline and a float64 one agree to 6e-8: the golden float32 tolerance, no outliers. The stored map reaches the tables as it is, and its tied texels sit on plateaus: a one-ulp change of the INPUT moves the output by up to 8e-2 through the 1e-9 ladder, so hand the function exactly the stored values. Cov: the isotropic generated map at 64x32 (8-bit preview), Earth 128x64 (stored u8 / 255), width ratio 0.5. strength 0: the matched layers carry weight 0, the result is the clipped sum of the original layers and residual (the map itself up to float32 rounding), and the final blend keeps it. Input convention: the u8 input(s) cov, earth are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

| `measure.cloudless` | `cloudgen.metrics.measure` | 5 | metrics.measure on a map: global_mean, zonal profile (5-degree bands: centers and means), area-weighted 10-bin histogram, the 56 gnomonic patches (patches) each measured by coherence, spectral_slope and object_stats, then coherence and spectral_slope = mean over patches (np.nanmean for the slope: NaN patches are skipped), coherence_by_lat = mean over the 8 longitudes of each of the 7 latitudes (PATCH_LATS order), big_object_share = mean of the per-patch shares, median_object_km2 = np.median over the 56 per-patch medians (even count: mean of the middle two), seam_ratio, pole_std. Extra outputs patch_coherence, patch_slope, patch_big, patch_median are the 56 per-patch values (patches order: latitude-major) recomputed by the case through the same calls, to localize a difference. A cloudless 64x32 map (all 0): every patch is flat, so spectral_slope is NaN (np.nanmean of an empty selection, with a warning), coherence 0, no objects (big share 0, median 0), seam_ratio 0 / 1e-12 = 0, pole_std (0, 0), the histogram all in the first bin. A flat patch (every texel exactly 0) has no power: its spectral slope is NaN, np.nanmean skips it, its coherence and objects are 0.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `measure.earth256` | `cloudgen.metrics.measure` | 35 | metrics.measure on a map: global_mean, zonal profile (5-degree bands: centers and means), area-weighted 10-bin histogram, the 56 gnomonic patches (patches) each measured by coherence, spectral_slope and object_stats, then coherence and spectral_slope = mean over patches (np.nanmean for the slope: NaN patches are skipped), coherence_by_lat = mean over the 8 longitudes of each of the 7 latitudes (PATCH_LATS order), big_object_share = mean of the per-patch shares, median_object_km2 = np.median over the 56 per-patch medians (even count: mean of the middle two), seam_ratio, pole_std. Extra outputs patch_coherence, patch_slope, patch_big, patch_median are the 56 per-patch values (patches order: latitude-major) recomputed by the case through the same calls, to localize a difference. The 256x128 Earth reference (smooth: its patches are ~18x upsampled, coherence ~0.84, slope ~-5.1). Tolerances, measured by moving every texel of the map by -1, 0 or +1 float32 ulp at random (6 trials on each of four maps, Earth at 256 and 512, an isotropic and a stirred generated map) and by promoting the map to float64, each set to about 10x the largest change seen: global_mean 5e-9, zonal 1.2e-7 and seam_ratio 2.4e-7 and pole_std 8e-8 (float32 row statistics: the float32 default); aggregated coherence 1.5e-9 and coherence_by_lat 5.3e-9 (1e-7 relative), spectral_slope 1.2e-8 (3e-8 relative), per-patch coherence 2.7e-8 (5e-7) and per-patch slope 3.2e-7 (1e-6): the sensitivity of a float64 chain to float32 rounding of the map, which a port that samples its patches in float64 never sees; hist and the bin ties: see area_histogram (1e-9 holds for a port that sends a texel equal to a float32 edge to the upper bin, which u8 / 255 maps do for 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8); the per-patch big share and median are hard thresholds (a texel crossing 0.5 can move a whole object across 1e5 km^2; no flip occurred in 24 nudged runs, the largest change was 3.8e-6) and carry a `frac` of 0.05 (2 of 56 patches may differ), the aggregated values the default floor.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `measure.half_cloudless.64x32` | `cloudgen.metrics.measure` | 5 | metrics.measure on a map: global_mean, zonal profile (5-degree bands: centers and means), area-weighted 10-bin histogram, the 56 gnomonic patches (patches) each measured by coherence, spectral_slope and object_stats, then coherence and spectral_slope = mean over patches (np.nanmean for the slope: NaN patches are skipped), coherence_by_lat = mean over the 8 longitudes of each of the 7 latitudes (PATCH_LATS order), big_object_share = mean of the per-patch shares, median_object_km2 = np.median over the 56 per-patch medians (even count: mean of the middle two), seam_ratio, pole_std. Extra outputs patch_coherence, patch_slope, patch_big, patch_median are the 56 per-patch values (patches order: latitude-major) recomputed by the case through the same calls, to localize a difference. The fast twin of the slow measure.half_cloudless, at 64x32: the Earth reference box-averaged to 64 wide with the western hemisphere (longitude -180..0) emptied to 0. The 21 patches centered at longitudes -135, -90 and -45 (every latitude) are exactly flat: their spectral slope is NaN and the aggregated spectral_slope is np.nanmean of the other 35 (np.mean would be NaN; patch_slope holds the NaN pattern). Several 5-degree zonal bands hold no row at this height and are NaN. Tolerances, measured by moving every texel of the map by -1, 0 or +1 float32 ulp at random (6 trials on each of four maps, Earth at 256 and 512, an isotropic and a stirred generated map) and by promoting the map to float64, each set to about 10x the largest change seen: global_mean 5e-9, zonal 1.2e-7 and seam_ratio 2.4e-7 and pole_std 8e-8 (float32 row statistics: the float32 default); aggregated coherence 1.5e-9 and coherence_by_lat 5.3e-9 (1e-7 relative), spectral_slope 1.2e-8 (3e-8 relative), per-patch coherence 2.7e-8 (5e-7) and per-patch slope 3.2e-7 (1e-6): the sensitivity of a float64 chain to float32 rounding of the map, which a port that samples its patches in float64 never sees; hist and the bin ties: see area_histogram (1e-9 holds for a port that sends a texel equal to a float32 edge to the upper bin, which u8 / 255 maps do for 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8); the per-patch big share and median are hard thresholds (a texel crossing 0.5 can move a whole object across 1e5 km^2; no flip occurred in 24 nudged runs, the largest change was 3.8e-6) and carry a `frac` of 0.05 (2 of 56 patches may differ), the aggregated values the default floor.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

| `patches.earth256.full` | `cloudgen.metrics.patches` | 160 | metrics.patches: 7 latitudes x 8 longitudes = 56 gnomonic patches of 256x256 (half-angle 10 deg) sampled bilinearly from the float32 map (stored here as u8, converted u8 / 255 in float32), returned as float64 (the lerp weights are float64). Outputs: the integer lat and lon of each patch, and the patches sampled at the texels listed in params.stride_idx (a subset, to keep the case small: a port computes the whole patch and compares these texels). Full-resolution float64 texels (rows and columns 64..191, the central 128x128 crop) of patch 0 (latitude -55, longitude -180): the patch center is ON the antimeridian, so the crop straddles it and columns wrap through the last map column. Same 1e-9. Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `patches.earth256.samples` | `cloudgen.metrics.patches` | 76 | metrics.patches: 7 latitudes x 8 longitudes = 56 gnomonic patches of 256x256 (half-angle 10 deg) sampled bilinearly from the float32 map (stored here as u8, converted u8 / 255 in float32), returned as float64 (the lerp weights are float64). Outputs: the integer lat and lon of each patch, and the patches sampled at the texels listed in params.stride_idx (a subset, to keep the case small: a port computes the whole patch and compares these texels). The 256x128 Earth reference: low-resolution texels interpolated ~18x, so the patches are smooth. Patches at longitude -180 straddle the antimeridian (the lerp wraps columns) and the 55 degree rows reach 65 degrees of latitude (rows clamp only beyond the poles, never here). The geometry is float64 (sin, cos, tan, arctan2, arcsin, floor), so a port is within 1e-9 (probe: relative noise of 4 ulp on the sampled directions moves a texel by <= 2.1e-13). Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |

| `spectral_slope.bound_ties` | `cloudgen.metrics.spectral_slope` | 8 | Log-log slope of the radially averaged power spectrum: (patch - mean) * Hann (np.hanning(n) outer itself, symmetric, 0.5 - 0.5 cos(2 pi i / (n - 1))), np.fft.fft2 (unnormalized, complex128), fftshift, /F/^2, radius r = int(hypot(x, y)) with x, y counted from n // 2 (truncation), radial[r] = mean of the power at integer radius r (np.bincount), wavelength of bin k = n * patch_px_km() / k km; bins with lo <= wavelength <= hi (inclusive) and radial > 0 enter np.polyfit(log k, log radial, 1), whose slope is returned; fewer than 4 bins give NaN. n = patch.shape[0] (square patches only). BIT-EXACT TIES on both bounds: lo_km = 32 * px / 8 = 35.10559981542595 and hi_km = 32 * px / 2 = 140.4223992617038 are the wavelengths of the bins k = 8 and k = 2 exactly, and the band is inclusive on both sides (>= lo, <= hi), so the fit uses the 7 bins k = 2..8; an exclusive bound would drop one end (6 or 5 bins, another slope). The tie holds only if the port evaluates n * px / k with the SAME px bits as Python (params.px_km_hex, = 2 tan(10 deg) 6371 / 256): a libm whose tan differs by one ulp turns it into a strict inequality on one side. Define patch_px_km in the port as that constant to be bit-exact here; the patch_px_km case itself only asks 1e-12. The whole chain is float64 (the Hann window is float64 even for a float32 patch): an independent float64 reimplementation agrees to 9e-16 with np.fft and to 1.4e-13 with an explicit DFT-matrix product (a different operation order; measured on Earth patches, noise and smooth fields), a float64 one-ulp nudge of the texels moves the slope by <= 1.7e-15; tolerance 1e-9.  |
| `spectral_slope.constant.nan` | `cloudgen.metrics.spectral_slope` | 8 | Log-log slope of the radially averaged power spectrum: (patch - mean) * Hann (np.hanning(n) outer itself, symmetric, 0.5 - 0.5 cos(2 pi i / (n - 1))), np.fft.fft2 (unnormalized, complex128), fftshift, /F/^2, radius r = int(hypot(x, y)) with x, y counted from n // 2 (truncation), radial[r] = mean of the power at integer radius r (np.bincount), wavelength of bin k = n * patch_px_km() / k km; bins with lo <= wavelength <= hi (inclusive) and radial > 0 enter np.polyfit(log k, log radial, 1), whose slope is returned; fewer than 4 bins give NaN. n = patch.shape[0] (square patches only). A constant patch (0.5: exactly representable, so the mean is exact whatever the summation order and the mean-subtracted power is exactly 0; a constant like 0.4 leaves a last-bit residual and a meaningless slope of rounding noise): radial > 0 never holds, NaN. |
| `spectral_slope.custom_band` | `cloudgen.metrics.spectral_slope` | 8 | Log-log slope of the radially averaged power spectrum: (patch - mean) * Hann (np.hanning(n) outer itself, symmetric, 0.5 - 0.5 cos(2 pi i / (n - 1))), np.fft.fft2 (unnormalized, complex128), fftshift, /F/^2, radius r = int(hypot(x, y)) with x, y counted from n // 2 (truncation), radial[r] = mean of the power at integer radius r (np.bincount), wavelength of bin k = n * patch_px_km() / k km; bins with lo <= wavelength <= hi (inclusive) and radial > 0 enter np.polyfit(log k, log radial, 1), whose slope is returned; fewer than 4 bins give NaN. n = patch.shape[0] (square patches only). Custom band lo = 30, hi = 1000 km on a smoothed 32x32 field: n * px = 281 km, so bins k = 1..9 (the k = 0 bin has infinite wavelength and never enters). The whole chain is float64 (the Hann window is float64 even for a float32 patch): an independent float64 reimplementation agrees to 9e-16 with np.fft and to 1.4e-13 with an explicit DFT-matrix product (a different operation order; measured on Earth patches, noise and smooth fields), a float64 one-ulp nudge of the texels moves the slope by <= 1.7e-15; tolerance 1e-9.  |

| `surface_contrasts.all_land` | `cloudgen.metrics.surface_contrasts` | 10 | surface_contrasts(img, land, deck): area-weighted (cos lat, float64) means of the map over masks with /lat/ < 60: land (land > 0.5, strict, in the dtype of land), ocean, 15..35 degree land and ocean, north, south, 40..60 north and south, the ocean-only north-minus-south pairs, the differences, and itcz_lat = the mean latitude of the 2.5-degree zonal-mean bands (/lat/ < 20) whose mean is >= max - 0.02; with a deck: ocean 8..35 degrees under a deck (deck > 0.3) and with none (deck < 0.05), both strict, compared in the deck's own dtype (a float32 deck against 0.3 compares float32(0.3): a texel equal to it is NOT under a deck). A mask with no texel gives 0 (the 1e-12 floor). The values come back in the order of params.keys. Maps are stored u8 and divided by 255 in float32 (land and deck as Surface.load does) unless the doc says otherwise. All land: the ocean masks (and the deck masks, which need ocean) are empty and read 0. Float64 formula on float32 or float64 inputs, summed by numpy (pairwise): against exactly rounded sums it differs by 2.7e-16 relative, so 1e-9. The itcz_lat crest picks bands by a hard threshold on float32 row means; params.itcz_threshold_margin is the smallest gap between a 2.5-degree band mean and the max - 0.02 threshold, > 1e-5 (a float32 rounding is 1e-7), so the selection is stable.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) img, land, deck are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
