| `surface_contrasts.generated.iso256` | `cloudgen.metrics.surface_contrasts` | 96 | surface_contrasts(img, land, deck): area-weighted (cos lat, float64) means of the map over masks with /lat/ < 60: land (land > 0.5, strict, in the dtype of land), ocean, 15..35 degree land and ocean, north, south, 40..60 north and south, the ocean-only north-minus-south pairs, the differences, and itcz_lat = the mean latitude of the 2.5-degree zonal-mean bands (/lat/ < 20) whose mean is >= max - 0.02; with a deck: ocean 8..35 degrees under a deck (deck > 0.3) and with none (deck < 0.05), both strict, compared in the deck's own dtype (a float32 deck against 0.3 compares float32(0.3): a texel equal to it is NOT under a deck). A mask with no texel gives 0 (the 1e-12 floor). The values come back in the order of params.keys. Maps are stored u8 and divided by 255 in float32 (land and deck as Surface.load does) unless the doc says otherwise. An isotropic generated map (256x128, 8-bit preview) on the same land and deck. Float64 formula on float32 or float64 inputs, summed by numpy (pairwise): against exactly rounded sums it differs by 2.7e-16 relative, so 1e-9. The itcz_lat crest picks bands by a hard threshold on float32 row means; params.itcz_threshold_margin is the smallest gap between a 2.5-degree band mean and the max - 0.02 threshold, > 1e-5 (a float32 rounding is 1e-7), so the selection is stable.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) img, land, deck are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `surface_contrasts.itcz_plateau` | `cloudgen.metrics.surface_contrasts` | 17 | surface_contrasts(img, land, deck): area-weighted (cos lat, float64) means of the map over masks with /lat/ < 60: land (land > 0.5, strict, in the dtype of land), ocean, 15..35 degree land and ocean, north, south, 40..60 north and south, the ocean-only north-minus-south pairs, the differences, and itcz_lat = the mean latitude of the 2.5-degree zonal-mean bands (/lat/ < 20) whose mean is >= max - 0.02; with a deck: ocean 8..35 degrees under a deck (deck > 0.3) and with none (deck < 0.05), both strict, compared in the deck's own dtype (a float32 deck against 0.3 compares float32(0.3): a texel equal to it is NOT under a deck). A mask with no texel gives 0 (the 1e-12 floor). The values come back in the order of params.keys. Maps are stored u8 and divided by 255 in float32 (land and deck as Surface.load does) unless the doc says otherwise. A zonal-only float32 map (no land) with a crest: bands at 0.60 and 0.585 are within 0.02 of the max (0.575 is not, 0.55 neither), so itcz_lat is the mean of their two centers (2.5), not the argmax (3.75). Float64 formula on float32 or float64 inputs, summed by numpy (pairwise): against exactly rounded sums it differs by 2.7e-16 relative, so 1e-9. The itcz_lat crest picks bands by a hard threshold on float32 row means; params.itcz_threshold_margin is the smallest gap between a 2.5-degree band mean and the max - 0.02 threshold, > 1e-5 (a float32 rounding is 1e-7), so the selection is stable.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) land are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `surface_contrasts.itcz_plateau_tie.f64` | `cloudgen.metrics.surface_contrasts` | 1 | surface_contrasts(img, land, deck): area-weighted (cos lat, float64) means of the map over masks with /lat/ < 60: land (land > 0.5, strict, in the dtype of land), ocean, 15..35 degree land and ocean, north, south, 40..60 north and south, the ocean-only north-minus-south pairs, the differences, and itcz_lat = the mean latitude of the 2.5-degree zonal-mean bands (/lat/ < 20) whose mean is >= max - 0.02; with a deck: ocean 8..35 degrees under a deck (deck > 0.3) and with none (deck < 0.05), both strict, compared in the deck's own dtype (a float32 deck against 0.3 compares float32(0.3): a texel equal to it is NOT under a deck). A mask with no texel gives 0 (the 1e-12 floor). The values come back in the order of params.keys. Maps are stored u8 and divided by 255 in float32 (land and deck as Surface.load does) unless the doc says otherwise. EXACT TIE of the plateau rule `band >= max - 0.02`: a float64 zonal-only map (72 rows x 2 columns: the row mean of two equal floats is exact) whose bands read 1.0 (3.75 degrees), 1.0 - 0.02 = 0.98 (1.25), 0.5 and 0.5: the 0.98 band equals max - 0.02 bit for bit (one IEEE subtraction) and is IN the plateau (>=), so itcz_lat = (3.75 + 1.25) / 2 = 2.5; a strict > gives 3.75. Float64 formula on float32 or float64 inputs, summed by numpy (pairwise): against exactly rounded sums it differs by 2.7e-16 relative, so 1e-9. The itcz_lat crest picks bands by a hard threshold on float32 row means; params.itcz_threshold_margin is the smallest gap between a 2.5-degree band mean and the max - 0.02 threshold, > 1e-5 (a float32 rounding is 1e-7), so the selection is stable.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) land are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `surface_contrasts.itcz_plateau_width` | `cloudgen.metrics.surface_contrasts` | 11 | surface_contrasts(img, land, deck): area-weighted (cos lat, float64) means of the map over masks with /lat/ < 60: land (land > 0.5, strict, in the dtype of land), ocean, 15..35 degree land and ocean, north, south, 40..60 north and south, the ocean-only north-minus-south pairs, the differences, and itcz_lat = the mean latitude of the 2.5-degree zonal-mean bands (/lat/ < 20) whose mean is >= max - 0.02; with a deck: ocean 8..35 degrees under a deck (deck > 0.3) and with none (deck < 0.05), both strict, compared in the deck's own dtype (a float32 deck against 0.3 compares float32(0.3): a texel equal to it is NOT under a deck). A mask with no texel gives 0 (the 1e-12 floor). The values come back in the order of params.keys. Maps are stored u8 and divided by 255 in float32 (land and deck as Surface.load does) unless the doc says otherwise. A zonal-only float32 map (72 rows, no land) whose 2.5-degree bands read 0.60 (3.75 degrees), 0.5801 (1.25), 0.5799 (-1.25) and 0.55: the plateau is the bands >= max - 0.02 = 0.58, so 0.5801 (0.0199 under the max) is in and 0.5799 (0.0201 under it) is out: itcz_lat = (3.75 + 1.25) / 2 = 2.5. A plateau width of 0.0202 or 0.0198 gives 1.25 or 3.75. Float64 formula on float32 or float64 inputs, summed by numpy (pairwise): against exactly rounded sums it differs by 2.7e-16 relative, so 1e-9. The itcz_lat crest picks bands by a hard threshold on float32 row means; params.itcz_threshold_margin is the smallest gap between a 2.5-degree band mean and the max - 0.02 threshold, > 1e-5 (a float32 rounding is 1e-7), so the selection is stable.  Input convention: the u8 input(s) land are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `surface_contrasts.odd_rows.equator` | `cloudgen.metrics.surface_contrasts` | 9 | surface_contrasts(img, land, deck): area-weighted (cos lat, float64) means of the map over masks with /lat/ < 60: land (land > 0.5, strict, in the dtype of land), ocean, 15..35 degree land and ocean, north, south, 40..60 north and south, the ocean-only north-minus-south pairs, the differences, and itcz_lat = the mean latitude of the 2.5-degree zonal-mean bands (/lat/ < 20) whose mean is >= max - 0.02; with a deck: ocean 8..35 degrees under a deck (deck > 0.3) and with none (deck < 0.05), both strict, compared in the deck's own dtype (a float32 deck against 0.3 compares float32(0.3): a texel equal to it is NOT under a deck). A mask with no texel gives 0 (the 1e-12 floor). The values come back in the order of params.keys. Maps are stored u8 and divided by 255 in float32 (land and deck as Surface.load does) unless the doc says otherwise. An ODD number of rows (73 x 16, float32 map and land): the middle row sits exactly on the equator (latitude 0.0, which the code builds as pi / 2 - 36.5 (pi / 73) = 0.0 in float64: a port must get exactly 0.0 here too). That row belongs to neither north (lat > 0, strict) nor south (lat < 0, strict), nor to the pairs built from them, but it is inside /lat/ < 60 and counts for land / ocean (it is bright, 1.0, and all ocean) and falls in the [0, 2.5) band of the zonal mean (>= 0 side): itcz_lat is the center of that band, 1.25. Float64 formula on float32 or float64 inputs, summed by numpy (pairwise): against exactly rounded sums it differs by 2.7e-16 relative, so 1e-9. The itcz_lat crest picks bands by a hard threshold on float32 row means; params.itcz_threshold_margin is the smallest gap between a 2.5-degree band mean and the max - 0.02 threshold, > 1e-5 (a float32 rounding is 1e-7), so the selection is stable.  |
| `zonal_profile.band7.offgrid` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 3 | Row means grouped in 7-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. A band that does not divide 180: edges run -90..92, so the last band extends past the pole (26 bands). Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). |
| `zonal_profile.earth256.band2_5` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 33 | Row means grouped in 2.5-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. 256x128 Earth, 2.5-degree bands (the width surface_contrasts uses): 72 bands. Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `zonal_profile.earth256.band5` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 32 | Row means grouped in 5-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. The 256x128 Earth reference (stored u8, u8 / 255 in float32), the default band: 36 bands. Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). Input convention: the u8 input(s) img are maps stored as bytes, and the function receives them as float32(u8) / float32(255) (`u8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0`: the division is done, and correctly rounded, in float32, as measure.load reads an 8-bit map), NOT as the raw bytes and NOT as a float64 quotient (a texel on a histogram edge or a threshold would move). params.u8_inputs lists them. |
| `zonal_profile.empty_bands` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 1 | Row means grouped in 5-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. 8 rows against 36 bands: most bands hold no row and are NaN, the rest hold one. Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). |
| `zonal_profile.f64.saturated_poles` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 7 | Row means grouped in 10-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. A float64 36x24 image (output stays float64), both pole rows saturated (0 and 1), 10-degree bands: two rows per band. Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). |
| `zonal_profile.one_band` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 1 | Row means grouped in 360-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. A band wider than the globe: edges (-90, 270), one band centered at 90 that holds every row (the mean of all row means). Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). |
| `zonal_profile.single_row` | `cloudgen.metrics.zonal_profile` | 0 | Row means grouped in 5-degree latitude bands (edges arange(-90, 90 + band, band), band k holds rows with a <= lat < b, lat = degrees of the row center); centers are the band midpoints. The smallest image, one row at latitude 0 (grid_lonlat: 90 - 0.5 * 180): only the [0, 5) band is not NaN. The row latitude is exactly 0.0 (pi / 2 - 0.5 pi), a tie that resolves the same in any language: a >= 0 goes to the band starting at 0. Centers are float64 closed-form (1e-9). The profile is a mean of float32 rows (float32 pairwise accumulation, stored dtype follows the image): float32 tolerance, measured 8e-8 absolute (1.3e-7 relative) against the same rows promoted to float64. An empty band is NaN (numpy warns). |
| `zone_index.f32.hadley30` | `cloudgen.match._zone_index` | 1 | Zone number of each row: floor(_earth_lat / 5).astype(int) (floor, then a truncating cast: non-negative here), so zones 0..13, the cap rows all in 13. No latitude sits within 1e-4 of a zone edge (a tie would depend on the last bit of degrees()), apart from exact 0 and the capped 65. A float32 latitude array (float32 arithmetic before the floor). |
| `zone_index.hadley15` | `cloudgen.match._zone_index` | 1 | Zone number of each row: floor(_earth_lat / 5).astype(int) (floor, then a truncating cast: non-negative here), so zones 0..13, the cap rows all in 13. No latitude sits within 1e-4 of a zone edge (a tie would depend on the last bit of degrees()), apart from exact 0 and the capped 65. A body whose Hadley edge is at 15 degrees: zones stretch twice as fast, the cap starts at 32.5 degrees. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles, the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. |
| `zone_index.hadley30` | `cloudgen.match._zone_index` | 1 | Zone number of each row: floor(_earth_lat / 5).astype(int) (floor, then a truncating cast: non-negative here), so zones 0..13, the cap rows all in 13. No latitude sits within 1e-4 of a zone edge (a tie would depend on the last bit of degrees()), apart from exact 0 and the capped 65. Earth: x * 30 is the latitude itself, up to the cap. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles, the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. |
| `zone_index.hadley60` | `cloudgen.match._zone_index` | 1 | Zone number of each row: floor(_earth_lat / 5).astype(int) (floor, then a truncating cast: non-negative here), so zones 0..13, the cap rows all in 13. No latitude sits within 1e-4 of a zone edge (a tie would depend on the last bit of degrees()), apart from exact 0 and the capped 65. A slow rotator (edge at 60 degrees): the zones cover half the globe's span, none reach the cap. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles pulled in by 1e-3 rad (/lat/ = 90 would sit exactly on a zone edge for this edge), the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. |
| `zone_index.hadley8_5` | `cloudgen.match._zone_index` | 1 | Zone number of each row: floor(_earth_lat / 5).astype(int) (floor, then a truncating cast: non-negative here), so zones 0..13, the cap rows all in 13. No latitude sits within 1e-4 of a zone edge (a tie would depend on the last bit of degrees()), apart from exact 0 and the capped 65. A fast rotator (edge at 8.5 degrees): all but the tropics are capped at zone 13. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles, the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. |
| `zone_index.hadley90` | `cloudgen.match._zone_index` | 1 | Zone number of each row: floor(_earth_lat / 5).astype(int) (floor, then a truncating cast: non-negative here), so zones 0..13, the cap rows all in 13. No latitude sits within 1e-4 of a zone edge (a tie would depend on the last bit of degrees()), apart from exact 0 and the capped 65. Edge at 90 degrees (no storm track): zones 0..5 only. Rows: a 64-row grid then the poles pulled in by 1e-3 rad (/lat/ = 90 would sit exactly on a zone edge for this edge), the equator, +-0.0, 1e-9, 0.5, 1.2, 1.5 rad. |

## pipeline

| case | function | KB | what it checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bake.box_downsample.crop.f64` | `bake.box_downsample` | 6 | A float64 10x70 map to width 16: the factor is 70 // 16 = 4, the map is cropped to (10 // 4) * 4 = 8 rows and 68 columns first, so the output is 2x17 (NOT 16 wide: the remainder is dropped, not resampled). float64: rtol 1e-12. |
| `bake.box_downsample.f32` | `bake.box_downsample` | 34 | Box average of a 64x128 float32 map to width 32 (factor 4 -> 16x32). The mean stays float32. float32 mean of 16 values: rtol 1e-6. |
| `bake.box_downsample.passthrough.factor1` | `bake.box_downsample` | 2 | An 8x24 map and width 16: the factor is 1 (<= 1), the map comes back unchanged (the engine resamples it, bake does not). Exact. |
| `bake.box_downsample.passthrough.wider` | `bake.box_downsample` | 2 | An 8x24 map and width 64: the factor is 0 (<= 1), the map comes back unchanged (the engine resamples it, bake does not). Exact. |
| `bake.key_seed` | `bake.key_seed` | 0 | zlib.crc32 of the UTF-8 bytes masked with 0x7FFFFFFF, for 18 keys (empty, ASCII, accents, an emoji, a long one); crc32 holds the unmasked value so the mask is checked (several keys have the top bit set). Exact: integers. Keys are in params.keys. |
| `bake.library_body` | `bake.library_body` | 1 | The library bodies at the four class centers and at edge cases: below the minimum edge (5 -> clamped to 10), above the maximum (120 -> 90), a zero center (no spin: edge 90) and a negative one (retrograde: the edge uses /period/). Outputs the body (period, radius, gravity, scale height) and the Hadley edge it lands on (equal to the center inside [10, 90]). Closed-form float64: rtol 1e-9. The class table, LIBRARY_SCALE_HEIGHT_KM and SLICE_WIDTH are module constants a port reads from bake.py (params.classes). |
| `bake.slug` | `bake.slug` | 0 | slug(key) = key.replace(":", "_").replace(" ", "_").lower() for the same 18 keys: str.lower is the full Unicode lowercase (accents, the final sigma of "ΑΣ" -> "ας", "İ" -> "i" + U+0307, "ß" unchanged). Output: the UTF-8 bytes of the slugs back to back and the i32 offsets (n + 1 of them). Exact. |
| `bake.write_entry.box` | `bake.write_entry` | 28 | One bake entry (inputs cov and extras.*), files decoded: hires = round(clip(cov) * 255) south-up (gunzipped), slice = round(box_downsample(cov, SLICE_WIDTH) * 255) (no clip: cov must be in [0, 1]), highShare = round(clip(box(high_share)) * 254), type = round(clip(box(type)) * 255), wind = encode_wind at its own grid. SLICE_WIDTH is set to 16 for this case (the real 2048 needs a map wider than 4096): the box factor is 4, the output 8x16. The inputs are dyadic (multiples of 1/512, block means exact in float32), with blocks whose mean is a rounding tie (0.5 x 255 = 127.5 -> 128, 0.25 x 254 = 63.5 -> 64, 0.75 x 254 = 190.5 -> 190). Exact: integers. params.record is the manifest record (file names are part of the contract). |
| `bake.write_entry.passthrough` | `bake.write_entry` | 33 | One bake entry (inputs cov and extras.*), files decoded: hires = round(clip(cov) * 255) south-up (gunzipped), slice = round(box_downsample(cov, SLICE_WIDTH) * 255) (no clip: cov must be in [0, 1]), highShare = round(clip(box(high_share)) * 254), type = round(clip(box(type)) * 255), wind = encode_wind at its own grid. SLICE_WIDTH is the real 2048: the 32x64 map is narrower, so slice, highShare and type are the map itself (box_downsample returns it). The inputs are dyadic (multiples of 1/512, block means exact in float32), with blocks whose mean is a rounding tie (0.5 x 255 = 127.5 -> 128, 0.25 x 254 = 63.5 -> 64, 0.75 x 254 = 190.5 -> 190). Exact: integers. params.record is the manifest record (file names are part of the contract). |
| `encode_wind.f32` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 3 | encode_wind on a f32 (10, 24, 2) wind of odd quarters of m/s (128 + v / 0.5 is an exact .5: half-to-even, 128.5 -> 128 but 129.5 -> 130), the clip edges +-63.25, +-63.75, +-64.25, +-1000, zero and negative zero: bytes clip(round(128 + v / WIND_STEP_MPS), 0, 255), B = 0. Exact. |
| `encode_wind.f64` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 4 | encode_wind on a f64 (10, 24, 2) wind of odd quarters of m/s (128 + v / 0.5 is an exact .5: half-to-even, 128.5 -> 128 but 129.5 -> 130), the clip edges +-63.25, +-63.75, +-64.25, +-1000, zero and negative zero: bytes clip(round(128 + v / WIND_STEP_MPS), 0, 255), B = 0. Exact. |
| `export.write_map.f32` | `cloudgen.export.write_map` | 4 | write_map on a 24x20 f32 coverage made of rounding ties: every (k + 0.5) / 255 (np.round is half-to-even: 127.5 -> 128, 126.5 -> 126; the product c * 255 stays in the input dtype), a third of them one f32 ulp below and above, the 16-bit ties (m + 0.5) / 65535, negative zero, values out of [0, 1] and infinities (np.clip first). Decoded files: the 16-bit master (I;16 PNG, north-up, round(c * 65535)), the 8-bit preview (L PNG, north-up, round(c * 255)) and the raw R8 gunzipped (south-up: the preview's rows reversed). Exact: integers. |
| `export.write_map.f64` | `cloudgen.export.write_map` | 6 | write_map on a 24x20 f64 coverage made of rounding ties: every (k + 0.5) / 255 (np.round is half-to-even: 127.5 -> 128, 126.5 -> 126; the product c * 255 stays in the input dtype), a third of them one f64 ulp below and above, the 16-bit ties (m + 0.5) / 65535, negative zero, values out of [0, 1] and infinities (np.clip first). Decoded files: the 16-bit master (I;16 PNG, north-up, round(c * 65535)), the 8-bit preview (L PNG, north-up, round(c * 255)) and the raw R8 gunzipped (south-up: the preview's rows reversed). Exact: integers. |
| `export.write_products.full` | `cloudgen.export.write_products` | 7 | write_products with type, high_share and wind: type = round(clip(a, 0, 1) * 255) as an L PNG, high_share the same x 254 (255 means "none authored" in the engine, so 1.0 maps to 254), wind an RGB PNG through encode_wind (R east, G north, B 0). The inputs are rounding ties, clip edges and infinities (see write_map); wind sits on odd quarters (128 + v / 0.5 is an exact .5). Exact: integers. params.products is the sidecar text. |
| `export.write_products.partial` | `cloudgen.export.write_products` | 2 | write_products with only high_share (the type and wind branches skipped): type = round(clip(a, 0, 1) * 255) as an L PNG, high_share the same x 254 (255 means "none authored" in the engine, so 1.0 maps to 254), wind an RGB PNG through encode_wind (R east, G north, B 0). The inputs are rounding ties, clip edges and infinities (see write_map); wind sits on odd quarters (128 + v / 0.5 is an exact .5). Exact: integers. params.products is the sidecar text. |
| `generate.cells_used` | `cloudgen.generate.generate` | 176 | fine_min_texels=0.1 and fine_frequency=24: the Worley cell scales of regime_texture that a 128 map resolves are 120 and 60 km (30 and 15 km are skipped), and the fine ladder has 3 octaves. This is the cell branch of regime_texture (taken only on maps 1024 wide or more at the default settings) reached end to end on a small map; worley3 and cell_edges plans enter the inputs. Frozen: coverage, fine, meta.fine_octaves, meta.flow_scalars, meta.hadley_edge_deg, meta.vortex_lat_deg, meta.vortex_lon_deg, streak. | `stat.earth.over_seeds` | `pipeline.stat.metrics_over_seeds` | 2 | Mean and sample standard deviation (ddof 1) of every metric of stat.earth.seed1..8 (Earth, 1024x512, seeds 1 to 8). A port that draws its own random tables must land inside mean +- 3 std on every scalar metric and every component of the vectors (zonal, hist, coherence_by_lat, pole_std): params.n_sigma (zonal_lat is the band centers: exact, std 0). The Python mean and std themselves are held to the per-seed tolerances (means) and 5 % (std: 8 seeds estimate a std to about 25 %, so a port must not read it as tighter than that). |
| `stat.earth.seed1.w1024` | `cloudgen.metrics.measure` | 1 | metrics.measure and compare() (against the Blue Marble reference) of the Python map of Earth, seed 1, 1024x512, default GenParams. A port draws its own random numbers, so this value is NOT a target for it (see stat.earth.over_seeds); it pins Python's own result. |

| `stat.earth.zonal_residual` | `pipeline.stat.zonal_residual` | 6 | The zonal mean coverage of the seed-1 Earth map in 5-degree bands (metrics.zonal_profile) against climate.target_zonal_coverage: the per-row calibration solves each row's mean to the target (bisection to 1e-9 on the unsmoothed thresholds), so what remains is the smoothing of the thresholds over 2 degrees, the sub-texel noise and the 5-degree banding. Outputs: the band centers, the map's band means, the target at the band centers, the target averaged over the rows of each band (the like-for-like value), the residual (band mean - band-mean target), the row-level residual (row mean - target at the row, 512 rows) and its largest absolute value, and the mean and std (ddof 1) of the band residual over seeds 1 to 8. A port keeps its band residual inside mean +- 3 std of the seeds. Measured on seed 1: the row residual has an rms of 0.017 and a maximum of 0.067 away from the poles, but the first and last rows of the map are far off (north pole rows 0 to 6: +0.15, -0.06, -0.34, -0.27, -0.17, -0.15, -0.19: a pole row is one point, so its coverage cannot follow its target, and the 2-degree smoothing of the thresholds spreads that across the neighbouring rows); the polar bands of the 5-degree profile carry the same residual (-0.078 in the last band). Tolerance: 10x the change of a band mean under +-1e-4 noise (1.1e-5) for the band values (2e-4), 1e-3 for the row residual (a row mean of 1024 texels). |
| `stat.reference.bluemarble` | `cloudgen.metrics.measure` | 1 | metrics.measure of the shipped Blue Marble cloud fraction (data/earth_cloud_fraction_bluemarble_2048.png, 8-bit, 2048x1024, /255 as measure.load reads it): what every compare() is taken against. Read by a port from the same PNG. |

## products_generate

| case | function | KB | what it checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `along.antipodal_nan` | `cloudgen.generate.along` | 0 | Degenerate input: back = -p (the first four points, exact negation) with f = 0.5 gives a zero vector, which normalizes to NaN (0 / 0 in IEEE, numpy warning suppressed); the last two points are ordinary. NaN equals NaN in the comparison; documents what the oracle does, a caller never passes an exactly antipodal back (the flow moves a point at most ~1 rad). Arguments: p and back (inputs, direction vectors of shape (..., 3)) and f (params): q = normalize(p (1 - f) + back f), with normalize(v) = v / //v// over the last axis (sphere.py) and NO epsilon guard: a zero vector gives 0 / 0 = NaN, not 0. The blend and the norm run in the dtype of the inputs (float32 vectors: float32 norm). Measured: float64 promotion changes the two finite points by at most 4.0e-8; the four antipodal points are discrete inputs (a one-ulp nudge would turn the NaN into a finite vector) and are not perturbed. |
| `along.grid_f32.f0p25` | `cloudgen.generate.along` | 72 | normalize(p (1 - f) + back f) with f = 0.25 (the generator's advect_fine) on the float32 grid directions of a 64x32 map and an analytic float32 back-trace (a latitude-dependent rotation about the axis plus a tilt): the vector blend, the norm and the division all run in float32. Tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: p and back (inputs, direction vectors of shape (..., 3)) and f (params): q = normalize(p (1 - f) + back f), with normalize(v) = v / //v// over the last axis (sphere.py) and NO epsilon guard: a zero vector gives 0 / 0 = NaN, not 0. The blend and the norm run in the dtype of the inputs (float32 vectors: float32 norm). Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 9.5e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.8e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 29x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `along.grid_f32.f0p6` | `cloudgen.generate.along` | 72 | normalize(p (1 - f) + back f) with f = 0.6 (the generator's advect_mid) on the float32 grid directions of a 64x32 map and an analytic float32 back-trace (a latitude-dependent rotation about the axis plus a tilt): the vector blend, the norm and the division all run in float32. Tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: p and back (inputs, direction vectors of shape (..., 3)) and f (params): q = normalize(p (1 - f) + back f), with normalize(v) = v / //v// over the last axis (sphere.py) and NO epsilon guard: a zero vector gives 0 / 0 = NaN, not 0. The blend and the norm run in the dtype of the inputs (float32 vectors: float32 norm). Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 1.0e-7; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.8e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 19x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `along.points.extrapolate` | `cloudgen.generate.along` | 9 | f outside [0, 1] is not clipped: f = 1.5 extrapolates past back (p (1 - f) + back f = -0.5 p + 1.5 back) before the normalization, and f = -0.5 the other way (second output). float32 points. Tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: p and back (inputs, direction vectors of shape (..., 3)) and f (params): q = normalize(p (1 - f) + back f), with normalize(v) = v / //v// over the last axis (sphere.py) and NO epsilon guard: a zero vector gives 0 / 0 = NaN, not 0. The blend and the norm run in the dtype of the inputs (float32 vectors: float32 norm). Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 1.2e-7; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 2.4e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 14x the larger of the two spreads. |

Tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: p and back (inputs, direction vectors of shape (..., 3)) and f (params): q = normalize(p (1 - f) + back f), with normalize(v) = v / //v// over the last axis (sphere.py) and NO epsilon guard: a zero vector gives 0 / 0 = NaN, not 0. The blend and the norm run in the dtype of the inputs (float32 vectors: float32 norm). Arguments: p and back (inputs, direction vectors of shape (..., 3)) and f (params): q = normalize(p (1 - f) + back f), with normalize(v) = v / //v// over the last axis (sphere.py) and NO epsilon guard: a zero vector gives 0 / 0 = NaN, not 0. | `area_mean.constant` | `cloudgen.generate.area_mean` | 1 | A constant field of 1.0 (float64): the weighted mean is 1 up to rounding (sum(c)/sum(c)), whatever the weights. rtol 1e-9. Arguments: a, the field (h, w), north-up. Weights: c_i = cos(lat_i) at the cell centres of grid_lonlat(w, h), row i at lat_i = 90 deg - (i + 0.5) 180 deg / h (row 0 is north, float64 radians pi/2 - (i + 0.5) pi / h); result = sum_i(mean_j a[i, j] c_i) / sum_i c_i, a float64 scalar stored as a one-element array. The row mean runs in the dtype of a (float32 rows are averaged in float32, numpy pairwise summation), the weighting in float64. Measured: one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 0 (worst of 6 random trials); no difference was observed at all. |
| `area_mean.f32.128x64` | `cloudgen.generate.area_mean` | 32 | Cosine-latitude weighted mean of a float32 (64, 128) field: numpy averages each float32 row in float32 (pairwise summation), then weights in float64. A port that accumulates in float64 differs by the float32 row-mean rounding; tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: a, the field (h, w), north-up. Weights: c_i = cos(lat_i) at the cell centres of grid_lonlat(w, h), row i at lat_i = 90 deg - (i + 0.5) 180 deg / h (row 0 is north, float64 radians pi/2 - (i + 0.5) pi / h); result = sum_i(mean_j a[i, j] c_i) / sum_i c_i, a float64 scalar stored as a one-element array. The row mean runs in the dtype of a (float32 rows are averaged in float32, numpy pairwise summation), the weighting in float64. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 3.3e-10; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 4.3e-9 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is about 1e3x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `area_mean.f64.128x64` | `cloudgen.generate.area_mean` | 64 | The same weighting on the float64 version of the field: closed form up to the summation order of the row means, rtol 1e-9. The row mean runs in the dtype of a (float32 rows are averaged in float32, numpy pairwise summation), the weighting in float64. | `area_mean.square.4x4` | `cloudgen.generate.area_mean` | 0 | A SQUARE (4, 4) float64 field a[i, j] = i + 0.1 j^2 (rows 67.5, 22.5, -22.5, -67.5 degrees): the only shape where a port that averages along the wrong axis (columns weighted by the latitude weights) does not fail on a shape mismatch but returns a wrong number, so the value depends on the row and the column differently. rtol 1e-9. Arguments: a, the field (h, w), north-up. Weights: c_i = cos(lat_i) at the cell centres of grid_lonlat(w, h), row i at lat_i = 90 deg - (i + 0.5) 180 deg / h (row 0 is north, float64 radians pi/2 - (i + 0.5) pi / h); result = sum_i(mean_j a[i, j] c_i) / sum_i c_i, a float64 scalar stored as a one-element array. The row mean runs in the dtype of a (float32 rows are averaged in float32, numpy pairwise summation), the weighting in float64. Measured: one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 2.2e-16 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is more than 1e5 times that spread. |

| `compose.all_f64` | `cloudgen.generate.compose` | 80 | The same call with every input promoted to float64, the arithmetic a float64 port performs: no float32 rounding is left in the expression, so the closed form is checked to rtol 1e-9. Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. Measured: one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 7.2e-16 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is about 2e4x that spread. |
| `compose.cumulus_clips` | `cloudgen.generate.compose` | 48 | cumulus_max = 1, a_fine = 1, fine up to +-1.6 and a sub-texel std of 3 (n_clouds = 1/9, outside the pipeline range on purpose): both the first clip (density (1 + a_fine fine) outside [0, 1]) and the one after the binomial term are taken at 0 and at 1; the first row holds fine = -1, 0, +1 at density exactly 1, where c is exactly 0 or 1 and sqrt(c (1 - c)) is 0. float32 fields, float64 threshold column, output float64; tolerance is the default float32 band with frac = 0.005 (the sqrt makes a few texels ill-conditioned, see the measurement). Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 1.4e-6; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 5.7e-5 (worst of 6 random trials); 0.05% of the elements leave the default band in the worst trial (the sqrt of c (1 - c) has an infinite slope where c reaches 0 or 1), so frac = 0.005 is 10x that. |
| `compose.no_streak_no_subtexel` | `cloudgen.generate.compose` | 32 | Defaults of the optional arguments: streak = 0.0 (scalar, so the shield texture is the constant 1 - 0.3 * 0.5) and subtexel = None (the binomial branch not taken), float32 fields, float64 threshold column. Tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 5.1e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.6e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 16x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `compose.pipeline_dtypes` | `cloudgen.generate.compose` | 48 | The pipeline call: float32 s, fine, streak (array) and subtexel (array), float64 threshold column t of shape (h, 1), default GenParams. Covers the shield ramp (both saturations), the shield texture (streak clipped at 0 and at 1), the cumulus density window and the binomial sub-texel term. The output is float64 but numpy rounds 1 + a_fine fine and 0.5 + 0.5 streak to float32 first, so a float64 port differs by those roundings; tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 5.8e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.9e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 14x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `compose.prm_variant` | `cloudgen.generate.compose` | 48 | Non-default GenParams (shield_ramp 0.1, shield_texture 0.6, cumulus_max 0.8, cumulus_depth 0.5, a_fine 1.4): every parameter is read from prm, none is a constant. Pipeline dtypes; tolerance is 3x the golden float32 default (the larger a_fine and the narrower ramp 0.1 amplify the roundings). Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 9.6e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 2.4e-6 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 13x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `compose.saturated_rows` | `cloudgen.generate.compose` | 48 | Alternating columns of s = +6 (shield saturated at 1) and -6 (shield 0 and cumulus 0: u below -1.6) with the sub-texel noise and an array streak <= -1 present: the coverage is exactly 1 and exactly 0 (the binomial term vanishes where the cumulus fraction is 0). Exact mode: every texel is 1 + 0 x c or 0 + 1 x 0, so any IEEE implementation returns exactly those floats. Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. |

Pipeline dtypes; tolerance is 2x the golden float32 default (a one-ulp change of s moves the narrow ramp by 3e-7 where the coverage is small). Arguments: s, fine, t, streak and subtexel are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted t, streak (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads (the others keep their defaults). params.result_dtype ("f64" or "f32") is the dtype numpy gives the result by promotion (a float64 t array or float64 inputs give f64, a python-float t with float32 fields keeps f32); it is informational, the stored output has that dtype. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 0; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.7e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 12x the larger of the two spreads. |

| `constants.products` | `cloudgen.products.constants` | 0 | PIN CASE, not a function: "cloudgen.products.constants" is a synthetic name for the module constants of products.py (no such function exists), and the OUTPUT arrays are the reference table, not a computed result. A port feeds them to nothing: its test asserts that its own constants equal these arrays. Contents: the MODIS high-share table (high_base_x, high_base_share), the type levels [closed, open, street, broken], [ADVECTION_DAYS, WIND_STEP_MPS, HIGH_COMMA_ASCENT, HIGH_DEEP_CORE] and the four Earth anchors of climate.py that zonal_wind_scale reads, [EARTH_ROTATION_DAYS, EARTH_RADIUS_KM, EARTH_GRAVITY, EARTH_SCALE_HEIGHT_KM]. Exact: they are literals, so the float outputs are compared bit for bit. The other cases of this group read these constants from the source (none is swept, none is repeated in their params). |
| `encode_wind.from_wind_map.64` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 22 | A realistic field: the (32, 64, 2) float32 output of wind_map for Earth with the ten vortices (stored as the input), encoded to RGB bytes (R east, G north, B 0). Exact bytes. |
| `encode_wind.near_ties.f32` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 2 | Values within 1 ulp .. 1e-4 of an exact tie, as float32: 128 + v / 0.5 is evaluated IN FLOAT32 (wind is float32), so a value a few 1e-6 above a tie is absorbed into the tie (the spacing of float32 near 128 is 1.5e-5) and rounds half-to-even, while a float64 evaluation would round it up: the same numbers in encode_wind.near_ties.f64 give different bytes. A port must add and round in the dtype of its input. Exact bytes. |
| `encode_wind.near_ties.f64` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 3 | The same numbers as encode_wind.near_ties.f32 promoted to float64 (exact promotion): now 128 + v / 0.5 keeps the 1e-6 offsets, so values above a tie round up and below it round down, and only the exact ties round to even. Compare with the float32 twin. Exact bytes. |
| `encode_wind.ramp_ties.f32` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 6 | v from -70 to +70 m/s in steps of 0.25 (float32, exactly representable), east ascending and north descending: every multiple of 0.25 that is odd lands on a .5 tie of 128 + v / 0.5, which np.round rounds half to EVEN (128.5 -> 128, 129.5 -> 130), and beyond +-64 m/s the clip saturates (63.75 -> 255.5 -> 256 -> 255; -64.25 -> -0.5 -> -0 -> 0). Blue is 0. Exact bytes. |
| `encode_wind.saturation.f32` | `cloudgen.products.encode_wind` | 0 | Saturation at both ends and special floats, float32: +-64 (256 -> 255, 0), 63.5 (255 exactly), 63.75 (255.5 -> 256 -> clip 255), -64.25, +-100, +-1e6, +-3e38 (v / 0.5 overflows float32 to infinity, the clip gives 255 and 0), +-inf, +0.0 and -0.0 (both 128). NaN is excluded: astype(uint8) of NaN is undefined. Exact bytes. |

Row 2: target 1.0 with a streak that dips the shield: unreachable, the mean is never above the target so the result runs down to the lower bound (about -10). Row 3: 0.95 with streak = +1 (shield capped at 0.7, coverage at 0.94): unreachable, lower bound. Row 4: target 1.0 with streak = -1, where the row IS exactly 1 at low t: mean > 1 is never true (strict comparison), so again the lower bound. Row 5: 0.999 with streak -1, reachable, near min(s) - 0.175 where the first texel leaves full shield. Rows 6 and 7: targets 1.2 and -0.2 (lower bound -10 and upper bound +10). Row 8: a small reachable 0.02. Row 9: 0.97 with streak -1, reachable. The bounds are exact up to the final bracket 20 / 2^36 (the result is the bracket midpoint); the target of rows 1 and 4 is exact, a port must compare strictly (>) in float64. Arguments: s, fine, target, streak, subtexel and offset are the inputs of that name when listed; an unlisted streak or offset (default 0.0) is the scalar in params, an unlisted subtexel is None; prm holds the GenParams fields compose reads. The 36 steps and the bracket [-10, 10] are literals of the source. | `surface_regime_weights.earth.hadley30` | `cloudgen.generate.surface_regime_weights` | 64 | Closed cells thinned over land (sea = 1 - continentality) and filled in over the decks (deck_window(x) x deck_strength), the open and street regimes scaled down by min(1, (1 - closed) / rest) where the decks take their share; float32 weights, continentality and deck strength, float64 latitude rows broadcast to the grid, Hadley edge 30. Columns 0..2 pin continentality / deck strength at (0, 0), (1, 1) and (0, 1); latitude rows reach both poles' neighbors. deck_window (surface.py) is evaluated inside: its own cases are in the texture_surface group. Outputs float32 from a float64 mix (the dtype chain is spelled out under Arguments below); tolerance is the golden float32 default for closed and twice that for the rescaled open and street shares, where the measured spread needs it. Arguments: the weights (w_closed, w_open, w_street = the dict entries closed, open, street), continentality and deck_strength (the two SurfaceTerms fields the function reads; the others are not read) and lat_rows (the latitude of each row in rad, broadcast over the columns as generate() does); hadley_deg is in params. Outputs closed, open, street (float32). Dtype chain to reproduce: sea = 1 - continentality and closed = w_closed sea are float32; deck = deck_window(x) deck_strength is float64 (x from the float64 latitudes); in closed + (1 - closed) deck the factor (1 - closed) is rounded to float32 BEFORE it meets the float64 deck, so closed (float64 from there on) can land a few 1e-8 above 1 and the scale (1 - closed) / rest then comes out slightly NEGATIVE (down to about -2.3e-8 on the open and street shares); the source does not clamp it and the stored outputs keep it. A float64 end-to-end port, or one that clamps the scale at 0, differs from this by at most that much (2.3e-8 on the stored cases), inside the tolerance: the negative shares are an artifact of that float32 rounding, not a contract. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 6.0e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.8e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 13x the larger of the two spreads. |

Tolerance as surface_regime_weights.earth.hadley30. Arguments: the weights (w_closed, w_open, w_street = the dict entries closed, open, street), continentality and deck_strength (the two SurfaceTerms fields the function reads; the others are not read) and lat_rows (the latitude of each row in rad, broadcast over the columns as generate() does); hadley_deg is in params. Outputs closed, open, street (float32). Dtype chain to reproduce: sea = 1 - continentality and closed = w_closed sea are float32; deck = deck_window(x) deck_strength is float64 (x from the float64 latitudes); in closed + (1 - closed) deck the factor (1 - closed) is rounded to float32 BEFORE it meets the float64 deck, so closed (float64 from there on) can land a few 1e-8 above 1 and the scale (1 - closed) / rest then comes out slightly NEGATIVE (down to about -2.3e-8 on the open and street shares); the source does not clamp it and the stored outputs keep it. A float64 end-to-end port, or one that clamps the scale at 0, differs from this by at most that much (2.3e-8 on the stored cases), inside the tolerance: the negative shares are an artifact of that float32 rounding, not a contract. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 6.0e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.4e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 14x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `surface_regime_weights.rest_scaling_edges` | `cloudgen.generate.surface_regime_weights` | 1 | Eight columns by four latitudes (21, 0, -21, 60 degrees; Hadley 30: the deck window is 1 at +-21 and 0 at the equator and at 60) taking every branch of the rest scaling. Columns as (closed, open, street, continentality, deck strength): rest = open + street = 0 (the guard rest > 1e-9 is false: scale 1), 5e-10 (under the guard: scale 1, nothing divided), 2e-9 (over it, 1 - closed over rest is huge and min(1, .) gives 1), a large rest 0.45 that the deck leaves little room for (partial scale 0.56 on the deck rows, min(1, 1.11) = 1 off the decks), closed = 1 with rest 0 (guard), closed = 1 with rest 0.5 (scale exactly 0: 1 - closed = 0), a half-land texel with rest 0.75 (scale 0.24 on the decks), and a full land texel (continentality 1: closed 0.9 becomes 0, then the deck fills it to 1 on the deck rows: scale 0). Out-of-contract sums (closed + rest > 1) are used on purpose where needed. Float32 outputs; the numerics include the float32 rounding of 1 - closed (spelled out under Arguments below). The guard threshold itself (1e-9) and the inner floor are pinned only between 5e-10 and 2e-9 here: the decades between 1e-9 and 1e-3 are surface_regime_weights.tiny_rest. Arguments: the weights (w_closed, w_open, w_street = the dict entries closed, open, street), continentality and deck_strength (the two SurfaceTerms fields the function reads; the others are not read) and lat_rows (the latitude of each row in rad, broadcast over the columns as generate() does); hadley_deg is in params. Outputs closed, open, street (float32). Dtype chain to reproduce: sea = 1 - continentality and closed = w_closed sea are float32; deck = deck_window(x) deck_strength is float64 (x from the float64 latitudes); in closed + (1 - closed) deck the factor (1 - closed) is rounded to float32 BEFORE it meets the float64 deck, so closed (float64 from there on) can land a few 1e-8 above 1 and the scale (1 - closed) / rest then comes out slightly NEGATIVE (down to about -2.3e-8 on the open and street shares); the source does not clamp it and the stored outputs keep it. A float64 end-to-end port, or one that clamps the scale at 0, differs from this by at most that much (2.3e-8 on the stored cases), inside the tolerance: the negative shares are an artifact of that float32 rounding, not a contract. Measured: promoting the float32 inputs to float64 changes the output by at most 1.5e-8; one-ulp nudges of every float input and parameter change it by at most 1.2e-7 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is 19x the larger of the two spreads. |
| `surface_regime_weights.tiny_rest` | `cloudgen.generate.surface_regime_weights` | 1 | The remainder rest = open + street from 9.9e-10 to 2e-3 against the room 1 - closed the decks and the land leave it, to pin the guard `rest > 1e-9` and the floor max(rest, 1e-9) of the denominator between 1e-9 and 1e-3, where an implementation with another constant (1e-6, 1e-3...) would pass every other case of this group: scale = min(1, (1 - closed) / rest) when rest > 1e-9 and 1 otherwise, so a remainder over the guard that closed leaves no room for is cut (to 0 when 1 - closed = 0) and a remainder under it is left alone. Two latitude rows (0 and 21 degrees; Hadley 30: the deck window is 0 at the equator, so closed = w_closed (1 - continentality), and exactly 1 at 21 degrees, so closed = c + (1 - c) deck_strength) share the 19 columns; column 0: rest 9.9e-10, just under the guard: scale 1; column 1: rest 1.02e-9, just over the guard: scale 0; column 2: rest 3e-9: scale 0; column 3: rest 1.5e-8 (open + street): scale 0; column 4: rest 1e-7: scale 0; column 5: rest 1e-6: scale 0; column 6: rest 1e-5: scale 0; column 7: rest 1e-4: scale 0; column 8: rest 5e-4: scale 0; column 9: rest 2e-3, over every threshold: scale 0; column 10: 1 - closed 6e-8 (1 ulp), rest 8e-8: scale 0.745; column 11: 1 - closed 1.2e-7, rest 5e-7: scale 0.238; column 12: 1 - closed 2.4e-6, rest 5e-6: scale 0.477; column 13: 1 - closed 2e-5, rest 5e-5: scale 0.399; column 14: 1 - closed 2e-4, rest 5e-4 (open 3e-4 + street 2e-4): scale 0.4; column 15: 1 - closed 1e-3, rest 1.3e-3 (just over 1e-3): scale 0.769; column 16: deck: closed 0.5 filled to 1 - 3e-8, rest 5e-8: scale 0.596 on the deck row; column 17: deck: 1 - closed 3e-9, rest 8e-9: scale 0.373 on the deck row; column 18: land: continentality 2^-13 = 1.2e-4, 1 - closed 1.2e-4, rest 2.4e-4: scale 0.508. The closed weights are exactly representable in float32 (closed = 1 - n 2^-24) and so is 1 - continentality (2^-13), so 1 - closed is exact whatever the precision; only the deck columns reach a 1 - closed under one float32 ulp, which needs closed in float64 (the dtype chain below: a float32 closed would give 0 or 6e-8 there). Oracle outputs are tiny (down to 2e-9) and several are exactly 0, so open and street are compared relatively with a 1e-12 floor: rtol 1e-5 (the golden float32 default), atol 1e-12. Tolerance experiment: the case is recomputed with every weight promoted to float64 (rest summed in float64 instead of float32, the only operation that differs); a one-ulp nudge of the inputs is NOT a valid experiment here, since it moves 1 - closed by 100% when closed is within an ulp of 1, which is the point of the case. Arguments: the weights (w_closed, w_open, w_street = the dict entries closed, open, street), continentality and deck_strength (the two SurfaceTerms fields the function reads; the others are not read) and lat_rows (the latitude of each row in rad, broadcast over the columns as generate() does); hadley_deg is in params. Outputs closed, open, street (float32). Dtype chain to reproduce: sea = 1 - continentality and closed = w_closed sea are float32; deck = deck_window(x) deck_strength is float64 (x from the float64 latitudes); in closed + (1 - closed) deck the factor (1 - closed) is rounded to float32 BEFORE it meets the float64 deck, so closed (float64 from there on) can land a few 1e-8 above 1 and the scale (1 - closed) / rest then comes out slightly NEGATIVE (down to about -2.3e-8 on the open and street shares); the source does not clamp it and the stored outputs keep it. A float64 end-to-end port, or one that clamps the scale at 0, differs from this by at most that much (2.3e-8 on the stored cases), inside the tolerance: the negative shares are an artifact of that float32 rounding, not a contract. Measured: promoting every weight to float64 changes closed by 0 and the open and street shares by at most 9.1e-8 relative (5.8e-11 absolute, none moves off a zero); the tolerance (rtol 1e-5) is 110x that relative spread. |
| `type_map.all_f32` | `cloudgen.products.type_map` | 40 | Float32 shield and float32 latitudes (a caller other than the pipeline): convective_tendency and the blend then run entirely in float32. Tolerance is the golden float32 default. Arguments: shield, the weights (w_closed, w_open, w_street = the dict entries closed, open, street) and lat_rows (the latitude of each row in rad, broadcast over the columns as generate() does with np.broadcast_to(lat[:, None], shape)); hadley_deg is in params. lat_rows is the CONVECTION latitude: generate() passes lat - radians(itcz_shift_deg) (the ITCZ-shifted latitude), not the grid latitude; this case takes it as given, applying the shift is the pipeline group's job. The four type levels and the other constants it reads are pinned in constants.products and read from the source. | `wind_map.earth.128` | `cloudgen.products.wind_map` | 192 | Earth, width 128, ten vortices (cyclones, anticyclones, tropical; two exactly on the antimeridian at +180 and -180, one on each pole). Flow.velocity (flow.py) and grid_dirs (sphere.py) are evaluated inside; their own cases are in the climate_flow and sphere_noise groups. The Flow is built by hand (hadley_deg, jet, trades, polar_easterlies and the vortex list in params, no random draw); wind_map ignores the flow's own jet, trades and polar_easterlies (they are non-zero here on purpose) and takes the climatological zonal wind of the BODY instead. Output east/north in m/s, float32 (height, width, 2), north-up, column 0 at -180. Computed in float64 and cast to float32 at the end: Tolerance is the golden float32 default: the field is computed in float64 and rounded to float32 once, so a different but equivalent float64 order of operations moves a texel by one float32 ulp at most (a port that computes in float32 throughout needs its own tolerance). The float64 intermediates are closed forms (exp, cross products, sums over the vortices): zonal rtol 1e-9 atol 1e-12, vortex_east and vortex_north rtol 1e-9 atol 1e-11 (an absolute floor in m/s for the texels where the vortices cancel). Arguments (all in params): width, hadley_deg, jet, trades, polar_easterlies, vortices (a list of {kind, lon, lat, radius, omega, ascent, age}: the Flow is rebuilt from them) and body (rotation_period_days, radius_km, gravity_mps2, scale_height_km, cloud_regime). Outputs: wind (the function result) and three intermediates that localize an error: zonal (h,), the climatological zonal profile of the grid rows in m/s (climatological_zonal_mps at the row latitudes, float64); vortex_east and vortex_north (h, w), the vortices' own flow projected on the local east and north in m/s (float64, before the zonal part is added to the east component). wind east = vortex_east + zonal[:, None] and wind north = vortex_north, cast to float32 (checked bit for bit when the case was frozen). Measured: one-ulp nudges of the float parameters change the outputs (the float32 wind and the float64 intermediates) by at most 7.5e-14 (worst of 6 random trials); the tightest tolerance band (rtol 1e-5 on the float32 wind, 1e-9 on the intermediates, with their atol) is 1.1e+03x that change. |

Outputs: wind (the function result) and three intermediates that localize an error: zonal (h,), the climatological zonal profile of the grid rows in m/s (climatological_zonal_mps at the row latitudes, float64); vortex_east and vortex_north (h, w), the vortices' own flow projected on the local east and north in m/s (float64, before the zonal part is added to the east component). wind east = vortex_east + zonal[:, None] and wind north = vortex_north, cast to float32 (checked bit for bit when the case was frozen). Measured: one-ulp nudges of the float parameters change the outputs (the float32 wind and the float64 intermediates) by at most 5.5e-14 (worst of 6 random trials); the tightest tolerance band (rtol 1e-5 on the float32 wind, 1e-9 on the intermediates, with their atol) is 1.1e+03x that change. |
| `wind_map.seam_vortex.minus_pi.64` | `cloudgen.products.wind_map` | 48 | A single cyclone exactly on the antimeridian (lon = -pi, lat 0.2 rad, radius 0.2, omega 3) on a body with no storm track (no zonal wind), width 64: the field is mirror-symmetric about the seam, east component of column 0 equal to that of column 63 and the north components opposite (checked exactly when the case was written), and +pi and -pi give the same map to within the 1.2e-16 of the center. Conventions as wind_map.earth.128 (float32 output computed in float64; the jet, trades and polar_easterlies of the flow are non-zero but ignored by wind_map). Tolerance is the golden float32 default: the field is computed in float64 and rounded to float32 once, so a different but equivalent float64 order of operations moves a texel by one float32 ulp at most (a port that computes in float32 throughout needs its own tolerance). The float64 intermediates are closed forms (exp, cross products, sums over the vortices): zonal rtol 1e-9 atol 1e-12, vortex_east and vortex_north rtol 1e-9 atol 1e-11 (an absolute floor in m/s for the texels where the vortices cancel). | `zonal_wind_scale.bodies` | `cloudgen.products.zonal_wind_scale` | 0 | Wind scale relative to Earth, g H / (Omega a) over Earth's value, for seventeen bodies in params.bodies: Earth (1), retrograde Earth (the same: /period/), a fast rotator, Mars-like, Titan-like and Venus-like (no storm track: 0), a 5-day Earth, two bodies either side of the raw Hadley edge 50 degrees that decides has_baroclinic_eddies (49.94 yes, 50.09 no), rotation None and 0.0 (no spin: 0), a 0.05-day rotator, negative gravity and zero scale height (g H floored at 0 so the scale is 0 although a storm track exists), and extreme radii. The eddies test is hadley_edge_raw_deg < 50 (strict; has_baroclinic_eddies of climate.py is evaluated inside, its own cases are in climate_flow). Float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9. Arguments: params.bodies, a list of body records (name is a label only). Measured: one-ulp nudges of the float parameters change it by at most 8.9e-16 (worst of 6 random trials); the tolerance is more than 1e5 times that spread. |
| `zonal_wind_scale.nonfinite_rotation` | `cloudgen.products.zonal_wind_scale` | 0 | Non-finite rotation periods (+inf, -inf, NaN) on an Earth-sized body: hadley_edge_raw is infinity for all of them (the test is `p is None or not isfinite(p) or p == 0`), so there is no storm track and the scale is exactly 0. Exact. Arguments: ONE body per element of the input array `rotation` (rotation_period_days; JSON params cannot hold infinities and NaN, so they are an input), the three other fields of the body shared in params (radius_km, gravity_mps2, scale_height_km, cloud_regime). Output: scale, one float64 per rotation (this case has no params.bodies). |

## sphere_noise

| case | function | KB | what it checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cell_edges.f0.seed7` | `cloudgen.noise.cell_edges` | 21 | Frequency 0: q = 0 for every point, so F2 - F1 is the same constant for the whole array (the F2 - F1 at the origin of the lattice). Output float32, shape = points.shape[:-1]. Chained float32 (matmul, scale, 27-neighbor search): recomputed in float64, with the matmul in both orders and with the points nudged by one float32 ulp either way: the largest observed difference is 5.5e-09 (it grows with the frequency because q is quantized to float32); tolerance rtol 1e-5 + atol 1e-06 (the golden.store floor, 180x). The Python casts the points to float32 first; that cast is not enforced (the tolerance was measured with float64 recomputes, so a float64 pipeline passes). The Python evaluates in chunks of 1 << 20 points: a port may chunk differently (checked at build time with chunks of 7 and 97 points: the same field within this tolerance). The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `cell_edges.f0p5.seed4` | `cloudgen.noise.cell_edges` | 28 | Frequency 0.5: the unit sphere spans at most 2 x 2 x 2 cells, so the field is smooth and slowly varying. | `dir_to_lonlat.edges.f64` | `cloudgen.sphere.dir_to_lonlat` | 3 | lon = arctan2(y, x), lat = arcsin(clip(z, -1, 1)), float64. The poles (x = y = +0.0: lon 0), the antimeridian (-1, +0.0, 0: lon = +pi), z beyond +-1 (1.0000001, -1.5, 2: clipped, no NaN), vectors that are NOT unit length (arctan2 ignores the length, arcsin uses z as is), the zero vector (lon 0, lat 0), tiny and huge components, a point 1e-12 below the pole (arcsin is ill-conditioned there: the input is exact, so only the implementation matters). Closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `dir_to_lonlat.f32` | `cloudgen.sphere.dir_to_lonlat` | 8 | float32 directions give float32 lon and lat (arctan2 and arcsin in float32). Includes the poles and the antimeridian, float32 z just below 1 (0.99999994, where arcsin is ill-conditioned but the input is exact) and z beyond 1 (clipped). Measured against the float64 evaluation of the same float32 inputs: 1.2e-7 in lon, 8.9e-8 in lat; tolerance 2e-6 abs + 1e-5 rel (more than 10x). |
| `dir_to_lonlat.grid3d.f32` | `cloudgen.sphere.dir_to_lonlat` | 2 | A (8, 16, 3) float32 array of directions (the grid_dirs of a 16x8 map): the output keeps the leading shape, lon and lat are (8, 16); they equal grid_lonlat up to float32 rounding (2e-6 abs, 1e-5 rel). |
| `dir_to_lonlat.signed_zero` | `cloudgen.sphere.dir_to_lonlat` | 0 | Signed zeros through arctan2 (IEEE atan2, as C99): y = +0.0 on the negative x axis gives lon = +pi and y = -0.0 gives -pi (both are the antimeridian: sample_equirect returns the same value for either); at a pole (x = y = 0) the longitude is 0, +pi, -0 or -pi by the signs of the zeros, which has no geometric meaning. Tagged degenerate: a port that does not reproduce signed-zero atan2 (WGSL does not guarantee it) may skip this case, as long as it treats both antimeridian cases alike. lat is exact. |
| `fade.f32` | `cloudgen.noise._fade` | 1 | The same curve on float32 t (a few float32 operations, the form Perlin3 uses). Measured against the float64 evaluation of the same inputs: 1.2e-6 relative at most (the polynomial has no root past the triple one at 0, so no cancellation beyond a factor 16); tolerance 1.2e-5 rel (10x), 1e-6 abs. |
| `fade.f64` | `cloudgen.noise._fade` | 2 | The Perlin fade curve 6t^5 - 15t^4 + 10t^3 evaluated as t*t*t*(t*(t*6-15)+10), float64, on [0, 1] (fade(0) = 0, fade(1/2) = 1/2, fade(1) = 1), beyond it (-2, 3, +-100: the polynomial is not clamped) and at 1e-8 from the ends. Closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `fbm.12oct.seed5` | `cloudgen.noise.fbm` | 6 | Twelve octaves from frequency 1.5 (the top octave at 1.5 * 2.03^11 = 3619 cycles per unit, where float32 coordinates near 3900 resolve 2.4e-4 of a cell): many octaves; the amplitudes fall as 0.5^k while the frequencies grow as 2.03^k, so every octave adds about the same absolute error. Chained float32: recomputed in float64, with the matmul written out in both orders, with an alternative lerp and with the points nudged by one float32 ulp either way: the largest observed difference is 4.3e-05; tolerance rtol 1e-4 + atol 0.00043 (10x). The Python casts the points to float32 and accumulates the octaves in float32; neither is enforced (the tolerance was measured with float64 recomputes, so a float64 pipeline passes). The Python evaluates in chunks of 1 << 20 points: a port may chunk differently (checked at build time with chunks of 7 and 97 points: the same field within this tolerance). The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `fbm.1oct.seed2` | `cloudgen.noise.fbm` | 9 | A single octave (the smallest legal count) from frequency 3, seed 2, at 512 unit directions (the first five are the poles and the axes): the plan has one amp 1.0, one rotation, one offset, and norm = 1 / PERLIN3_STD. | `fbm.stat.1oct.billow` | `cloudgen.noise.fbm` | 0 | Statistics of fbm over 200000 random points, 1 octave(s), billow True: pins the PERLIN3_STD / PERLIN3_ABS_MEAN / PERLIN3_ABS_STD normalization (the output is meant to have unit variance and zero mean). Per seed s = 0..15: 200000 points uniform on the unit sphere (seeded with 100 + s), frequency 40, fbm seed 7000 + s; the outputs are the 16 means and 16 standard deviations. Measured over the 16 seeds: mean +0.0206 (sd over seeds 0.015), std 1.0127 (sd 0.0105). The mean is not 0 for billow: E/noise/ on these points is about 1.5% above PERLIN3_ABS_MEAN (the constant was measured on points in a box; E/noise/ also varies by 0.5% between permutation tables), and the std is 1.3% above 1. A port draws its own points and tables: its mean and std over 200000 points (one value per run) must lie within params.expect.mean +- k_sigma * mean_sd and std +- k_sigma * std_sd (k_sigma = 4). The Python recompute is compared at rtol 1e-5 + atol 2e-6: float32 variants of the chain (float64, other matmul order, points nudged by one ulp) move a 200000-point mean by 1.3e-7 and the std by 6e-8 at most. |
