| `gnomonic_patch.antimeridian.east` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 2 | Centered on lon +pi at lat 0.5 rad: the patch straddles the antimeridian, the directions are plain 3D vectors so there is no seam (x < 0 throughout, y changes sign). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.antimeridian.west` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 2 | Centered on lon -pi: the same patch as antimeridian.east up to rounding (sin(+-pi) = +-1.2e-16). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.equator.n9` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 2 | A 9x9 patch of half angle 0.3 rad at lon 0, lat 0: the center sample (row 4, column 4) is (1, 0, 0); row 0 is north (z > 0), column 0 is west (y < 0). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.mid_latitude.n17` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 7 | A 17x17 patch at (-73, 41) degrees with the 10 degree half angle the metrics use (a 256x256 patch in production): the isotropic tangent-plane view; every sample is unit length. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.n1` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 0 | Smallest legal size, n = 1: np.linspace(-1, 1, 1) is [-1] (not 0), so the single sample is the NORTH-WEST corner of the patch (x = -tan, y = +tan), not the center. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.n2` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 0 | n = 2: the four corners of the patch (linspace(-1, 1, 2) = [-1, 1]); row 0 north, column 0 west. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.near_quarter_turn` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 1 | Half angle 1.5 rad (tan = 14.1, close to the 90 degree limit where the tangent plane goes to infinity): the corners are almost on the great circle 90 degrees away. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.north_pole.n9` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 2 | A patch centered on the north pole (lat pi/2, where the center is (6e-17 cos lon, 6e-17 sin lon, 1)): tangent_frame takes the pole fallback, east = (0, 1, 0), north = (-1, 0, 0), independent of lon0. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.pole_threshold.inside` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 1 | Center 5e-10 rad from the pole: /z x c/ = 5e-10 < 1e-9, so the fallback frame is used although lon0 = 2 would give a different east: the frame jumps at the threshold. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.pole_threshold.outside` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 1 | Center 2e-9 rad from the pole: /z x c/ = 2e-9 > 1e-9, the regular frame (east = (-sin 2, cos 2, 0)) is used: the same patch as pole_threshold.inside, rotated by 2 rad about the pole. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.south_pole.n9` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 2 | The south pole: fallback east (0, 1, 0), north = (+1, 0, 0): row 0 points toward +x there. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.tiny_half_angle` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 1 | Half angle 1e-3 rad: a 5x5 patch whose samples differ from the center by up to 1.4e-3 (a port must not lose them to float32 rounding: this is float64). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.wide.1p2rad` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 2 | A wide patch, half angle 1.2 rad (tan = 2.57): the edge midpoints are 1.2 rad from the center and the corners 1.30 rad; directions are renormalized from the tangent plane, so the samples bunch toward the corners. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `gnomonic_patch.zero_half_angle` | `cloudgen.sphere.gnomonic_patch` | 0 | Half angle 0: tan = 0, every sample is the center direction (up to normalization rounding). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `grad.all_16_branches` | `cloudgen.noise._grad` | 1 | The gradient selector on h & 15: for h = 0..15 with (x, y, z) = (0.3, 0.5, 0.7) the 16 results are {+-x or +-y, +-y or +-x or +-z} per the improved-Perlin table: u = x if h < 8 else y; v = y if h < 4, x if h in {12, 14}, else z; the sign of u flips on bit 0 of h and the sign of v on bit 1. The following rows use h = 16..47 and 240..255 (the mask & 15 must wrap them) with random coordinates. One float32 addition of two selected, possibly negated inputs: bit-identical in any float32 implementation; the tolerance (1e-6 rel) is only slack for a port that computes in float64. |
| `grid_dirs.16x8` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_dirs` | 2 | Unit directions of the texel centers of a 16x8 north-up equirectangular grid, float32 (z north). Computed in float64 and rounded to float32 once, so a float64 port matches within half a float32 ulp (6e-8); measured against the same grid with float32 trigonometry: 1.2e-7 at most, so the tolerance is 10x that (2e-6 abs, 1e-5 rel). |
| `grid_dirs.1x1` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_dirs` | 0 | Smallest legal grid, 1x1 float32: the single direction is (1, 0, 0) exactly (lon = lat = 0.0 exactly, see grid_lonlat.1x1). Absolute floor 2e-6. |
| `grid_dirs.f64.16x8` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_dirs` | 3 | The same 16x8 grid with dtype float64: the closed form of lonlat_to_dir at the texel centers, no rounding. Row 0 is the north pole edge (z = sin(pi/2 - pi/16)), column 0 is longitude -pi + pi/16 (x < 0, y < 0). Float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `grid_dirs.f64.64x32` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_dirs` | 48 | A 64x32 float64 grid: every row is a circle of latitude, every direction has unit length to 2.2e-16, and columns 0 and 63 are 2.8 degrees either side of the antimeridian, 5.6 apart (x < 0, y of opposite signs). Float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `grid_dirs.odd.7x5` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_dirs` | 0 | Odd sizes 7x5, float32: the centre texel is (1, 0, 0) and the rows are symmetric about the equator (z of row j = -z of row 4-j). Same tolerance as grid_dirs.16x8. |
| `grid_lonlat.16x8` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_lonlat` | 0 | Longitude and latitude (rad) of the texel centers of a 16x8 north-up grid: column 0 at -pi + pi/16, row 0 at pi/2 - pi/16, centers at (i + 0.5) steps. Closed-form float64: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12 (the middle columns sit at +-pi/W, a difference of two numbers near pi, so an absolute floor is needed). |
| `grid_lonlat.1x1` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_lonlat` | 0 | Smallest legal grid, 1x1: the single texel center is lon 0, lat 0, both EXACTLY 0.0 here (-pi + pi and pi/2 - pi/2). Same tolerance. |
| `grid_lonlat.4096x2048` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_lonlat` | 48 | A 4096x2048 map: 4096 longitudes and 2048 latitudes, float64 closed form (-pi + (i + 0.5) * (2 pi / W): the multiplication is before the addition, in this order). rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `grid_lonlat.7x5` | `cloudgen.sphere.grid_lonlat` | 0 | Odd sizes 7x5: the middle column and row sit at lon 0.0 and lat 0.0 (exactly, here), the other centers at multiples of 2pi/7 and pi/5 from them. Same tolerance. |
| `lonlat_to_dir.edges.f64` | `cloudgen.sphere.lonlat_to_dir` | 6 | x = cos(lat)cos(lon), y = cos(lat)sin(lon), z = sin(lat), float64, over the product of longitudes {0, +-pi, pi/2, +-1, +-1e3, 1e6, 7} (the antimeridian both ways, huge and unreduced angles) and latitudes {+-pi/2 (the poles, where cos is 6e-17, not 0), 0, +-0.3, 2.0, -3.0, pi (beyond the pole: cos < 0, no clamp)} plus 64 random pairs. Closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12 (the pole x, y are ~1e-17). |
| `lonlat_to_dir.f32` | `cloudgen.sphere.lonlat_to_dir` | 6 | float32 lon and lat give float32 output (all the trigonometry in float32, not in float64). The poles (float32 pi/2 is 1.5707964 > pi/2, so cos = -4.4e-8, not 0), the antimeridian and unreduced angles (1e3, 100.5) are included. Measured against the float64 evaluation of the same float32 inputs: 7.2e-8 at most; the golden.store default (1e-5 rel, 1e-6 abs) is more than 10x that. |
| `lonlat_to_dir.grid2d.f64` | `cloudgen.sphere.lonlat_to_dir` | 2 | Two-dimensional lon and lat (a 5x9 meshgrid with both poles and both antimeridian columns, -pi and +pi): the output stacks the three components on a new LAST axis, shape (5, 9, 3). Closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `map_max_frequency.defaults` | `cloudgen.noise.map_max_frequency` | 0 | The function called WITHOUT min_texels: the Python default 4.0 (4 texels per wavelength at the equator), so fmax = width / (8 pi): widths 0 (gives 0), 1, 7, 64, 256, 512, 1000, 4096, 8192, 16384, 65536 and 1e9. A default of 3 or 2 changes every nonzero row by 33% or 100%. Closed-form float64: rtol 1e-9. |
| `map_max_frequency.table` | `cloudgen.noise.map_max_frequency` | 0 | width / (2 pi min_texels): the highest noise frequency (cycles per radian) a width-W map resolves with at least min_texels texels per wavelength, 15 rows: widths 0 (gives 0), 1 .. 1e9, min_texels 0.25 .. 8 and the default 4, one negative min_texels (sign carried through). Every row passes min_texels explicitly (the rows with 4 just happen to equal the default): the default itself is pinned by map_max_frequency.defaults. Closed-form float64: rtol 1e-9. |
| `normalize.degenerate.f32` | `cloudgen.sphere.normalize` | 0 | Unguarded degenerate input, float32: the zero vector gives NaN (0/0); 1e30 squares to infinity so the result is 0, not 1; 1e-30 squares to 0 (below the smallest float32 subnormal) so the result is [inf, NaN, NaN]; 1e20 each overflows the sum. These are what the Python does with the naive formula; no caller passes such vectors. Tagged degenerate: a port that guards (hypot, scaling) may differ. |
| `normalize.degenerate.f64` | `cloudgen.sphere.normalize` | 0 | The same in float64: zero -> NaN, 1e200 squares to infinity -> 0, 1e-200 squares to 0 -> [inf, NaN, NaN], 1e160 each overflows the sum -> 0. Tagged degenerate. |
| `normalize.f32` | `cloudgen.sphere.normalize` | 1 | float32 input stays float32 (the norm is accumulated in float32, x*x + y*y + z*z in that order); components 1e-15 and 1e15 are as extreme as float32 squares allow. Error against the float64 evaluation is 1.0e-7 relative (6.4e-8 absolute); default tolerance (1e-5 rel, 1e-6 abs) = 100x that. |
| `normalize.f64` | `cloudgen.sphere.normalize` | 1 | v / //v// along the last axis, float64: axis-aligned, negative, 1e-100 and 1e100 components (the squares stay inside float64), random vectors of magnitude 1e-30..1e30. The norm is sqrt(x*x + y*y + z*z) summed in that order. Closed form: rtol 1e-9. |
| `normalize.f64.ndim3` | `cloudgen.sphere.normalize` | 1 | The same on a (3, 4, 3) array: the norm is taken along the last axis only (keepdims), so every one of the 12 vectors becomes unit length independently. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `octave_ladder.defaults` | `cloudgen.noise.octave_ladder` | 0 | The function called WITHOUT lacunarity and top: the Python defaults 2.03 and 0.95 (texture.py calls octave_ladder(fine_frequency, fmax) with exactly these; generate.py passes top). 16 rows (low_frequency, max_frequency): the production maps (low 48 at widths 256..16384 with 2 texels per wavelength, low 3 at 256 and 4096 with 4), hi == low (10 against 10 / 0.95: both sides of the comparison give (10, 1) because ln(1 + 2e-16) / ln(2.03) floors to 0), hi just above low, hi below low, low 0.01 .. 1e4 (20 octaves). No row sits within 1e-6 of a floor boundary of the log ratio (checked at build time). Both the start frequency (hi / lacunarity^(n - 1), rtol 1e-9) and n move with either default: five mutated defaults (lacunarity 2.0, 2.029, 2.05, top 0.9, 0.951) each change at least 8 of the 16 rows (checked at build time). start: float64 closed form, rtol 1e-9; n: exact. |
| `octave_ladder.fragile` | `cloudgen.noise.octave_ladder` | 0 | hi / low is EXACTLY lacunarity^3, so the floor of ln(ratio) / ln(lacunarity) sits on the integer 3: with lacunarity 2 numpy gives 3.0 (n = 4, start 1) but with 2.03 it gives 2.9999999999999996 (n = 3, start 2.03, although four octaves 1, 2.03, 4.12, 8.37 fit). The result depends on how ln rounds, so another math library can land on the other side. Tagged fragile: it pins the Python result. n exact, start float64 closed form (rtol 1e-9). |
| `octave_ladder.table` | `cloudgen.noise.octave_ladder` | 1 | A ladder anchored at the top: hi = top * max_frequency; when hi <= low_frequency the result is (hi, 1), else n = floor(ln(hi / low) / ln(lacunarity)) + 1 octaves and the start frequency hi / lacunarity^(n - 1) (so the last octave sits at hi). 20 rows (low, max_frequency, lacunarity, top): the production maps (low 48 at widths 512..16384, low 3 at 256 and 4096), hi == low, hi just above low, hi below low, hi far above (low 0.01, max 1e4 -> 20 octaves), lacunarity 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4 and top 0.5..1.2. No row sits within 1e-6 of a floor boundary of the log ratio (checked at build time; see octave_ladder.fragile). Every row passes lacunarity and top explicitly; the Python defaults (2.03 and 0.95, what texture.py uses) are pinned by octave_ladder.defaults. start: float64 closed form, rtol 1e-9; n: exact. |
| `octaves_below.defaults` | `cloudgen.noise.octaves_below` | 0 | The function called WITHOUT its lacunarity argument: the Python default 2.03 (the value every caller relies on). 19 rows (frequency, max_frequency): for k = 2, 5, 10, 20, 40 one row 0.1% above 2.03^(k - 1) and one 0.1% below 2.03^k (frequency 1), so the count is k and any default off by 0.05% or more (2.029 and 2.031 are tried) changes at least 4 rows; plus production-like rows (48 .. 163 and 326, 3 .. 24, 0.5 .. 1000), frequency just above and just below the maximum (5 against 5.005 and 4.995: n = 1 either way, the second from the clamp of 0 to 1), frequency above the maximum and a very low frequency. Every row is at least 5e-4 (relative) from a boundary 2.03^j * frequency (checked at build time). Integer output: exact. |
| `octaves_below.fragile` | `cloudgen.noise.octaves_below` | 0 | max_frequency set to frequency * 2.03^k computed in Python (pow): the loop compares frequency * 2.03^k < frequency * 2.03^k, which is False, so n = k (3 and 4 here). A port that raises to the power with repeated multiplication (Rust powi) instead of libm pow can round the other way and get k + 1. Tagged fragile: it pins the Python result, which depends on pow being the same function in both places. Integer output: exact. |
| `octaves_below.table` | `cloudgen.noise.octaves_below` | 1 | How many octaves frequency * lacunarity^k (k = 0, 1, ...) start strictly below max_frequency, at least 1: 20 rows (frequency, max_frequency, lacunarity). Covers frequency above and equal to the maximum (n = 0 raised to 1), one octave, many (35 for 1 .. 1e6 at lacunarity 1.5, 59 for 1e-9 .. 1e9), lacunarity 1.1 to 1000, and the exact boundary with lacunarity 2.0 (3 * 2^3 = 24 is NOT below 24, so n = 3; 24.000001 gives 4; both exact in float64). No other row is within 1e-9 of a boundary (checked at build time): see octaves_below.fragile. The function loops forever for frequency <= 0 or lacunarity <= 1 with frequency < max, so they are not cases. Every row passes lacunarity explicitly; the Python default (2.03, what the callers use) is pinned by octaves_below.defaults. Integer output: exact. |
| `perlin3.f32.identity_perm` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__call__` | 2 | A hand-made identity permutation (perm[i] = i): the corner hash is then (X + Y + Z) & 255, so sweeping the cell along each axis in turn (16 cells x 3 axes, fractions k/64 + 1/128) drives every gradient branch of the table in a predictable order. The permutation is the input plan0.perm (seed null). Same tolerance as perlin3.f32.random (measured spread 1.1e-7). The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `perlin3.f32.large` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__call__` | 6 | Large coordinates, /x/ from 1e4 to 1e7 (float32 resolution 1e-3 to 1): the fractional part x - floor(x) is exact in float32 but coarse, and the int64 cast & 255 must see the whole integer part. Measured spread against float64: 4.8e-7. Same tolerance as perlin3.f32.random. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `perlin3.f32.lattice` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__call__` | 6 | Points ON the integer lattice (fractional parts exactly 0: the noise is 0 there, through grad(h, 0, 0, 0)), -0.0, and the same cells approached from either side by 2^-20 and 2^-10: floor() puts x - 2^-20 in the cell BELOW with a fractional part of 1 - 2^-20, so a port must floor, not truncate (coordinates up to +-300, negative half of the time, past the 256 wrap). Values are tiny (< 2e-3): the tolerance is the absolute one of the other Perlin cases (measured spread 1.9e-8). The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `perlin3.f32.random` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__call__` | 32 | Improved Perlin noise, float32, at 2000 random points in [-8, 8]^3 (cells of both signs), seed 6 (the permutation is the input plan0.perm). The 8 corner hashes of the points reach all 16 gradient branches (checked at build time). Output float32, /value/ < 0.8. Chained float32 (about 30 operations): measured against the float64 evaluation of the same inputs 1.2e-6 at most, against a float32 evaluation with another operation order (a*(1-t)+b*t) 1.9e-7; tolerance 1.5e-5 abs + 1e-5 rel (10x). The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `perlin3.f32.wrap` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__call__` | 5 | The lattice index wraps by & 255 (two's complement for negatives: -1 & 255 = 255): 80 base points with cells in 0..255 and fractions that are multiples of 1/64 (so the float32 coordinates are exact up to +-71000), repeated shifted by multiples of 256 on each axis (both signs, up to 70656), and once shifted into negative cells. Outputs are bit-identical across the three copies of a point (asserted when building the case). Same tolerance as perlin3.f32.random. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `perlin3.f64.random` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__call__` | 32 | The same kernel on FLOAT64 coordinates (cells of both signs up to +-300): every intermediate stays float64 and the result is float64, so a float64 port is checked tightly. It differs from the float32 evaluation of the same points by up to 1.2e-6 (see perlin3.f32.random). Closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `perlin3.table` | `cloudgen.noise.Perlin3.__init__` | 3 | The constructor doubles the 256-entry permutation to a 512-entry table (perm[i + 256] = perm[i], so the kernel can index p[A + 1] up to 511 without a wrap). The permutation is the recorded draw (an input: a port draws its own); the output is the doubled int32 table, exact. |
| `random_rotation.from_normal_matrix` | `cloudgen.noise._random_rotation` | 5 | QR of a 3x3 matrix g drawn from the normal distribution (g is the INPUT: the draw), then the columns of q are multiplied by sign(diag(r)) (which makes the factorization unique, so any QR algorithm gives this q), then column 0 is negated when det(q) < 0, so the result is a proper rotation (det +1). 34 matrices: the identity, -I, diag(1,1,-1) (the determinant flip), permutation and triangular ones with negative diagonals, 24 random normal matrices, and two scaled by 1e3 and 1e-3 (the rotation is scale free). The flip of column 0 is taken for 20 of the 34 and not for the others (checked at build time; condition numbers below 1e3). The Python function takes a numpy Generator and calls rng.normal(size=(3, 3)) once: the case replaces that draw by the input g (a port's equivalent is a function of g, params.rng_draw). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `random_rotation.recorded_orthonormal` | `property.cloudgen.noise._random_rotation` | 5 | A PROPERTY case (it never calls _random_rotation: the function field has the prefix property.; a port checks its own drawn rotations against the same two outputs). The 48 octave rotations recorded from two fbm calls (24 octaves each, seeds 101 and 102; float32, the inputs) are orthonormal with determinant +1: rot rot^T equals the identity and det(rot) = 1, both computed here in float64 from the float32 matrices (so they deviate by the float32 rounding: 7.3e-8 in the Gram matrix, 6.9e-8 in the determinant; the expected outputs are the identity and 1 to within 1e-6). A port that draws its own rotations must satisfy the same two outputs on them. |
| `sample_equirect.channels_1` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 2 | A (8, 16, 1) image: the ndim == 3 branch is channel-generic (the weights get a trailing axis of length 1 and broadcast over the 1 channel), the output is (60, 1) float64. 60 unit directions including the poles and the axes. float64, rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.channels_4` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 5 | A (8, 16, 4) image: the ndim == 3 branch is channel-generic (the weights get a trailing axis of length 1 and broadcast over the 4 channels), the output is (60, 4) float64. 60 unit directions including the poles and the axes. float64, rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.edges.f64` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 3 | float64 directions at the places that break a lookup: the poles (lat clamps to the first and last row; lon of the exact pole is arctan2(0, 0) = 0), the antimeridian (-1, 0, 0) and (-1, -0.0, 0): fx = -0.5 or W - 0.5, both average the last and first columns and give the SAME value (x0 % W wraps a negative index to W - 1: floor modulo, not truncation), the +-y axes, directions that are not unit length (lon is scale free, but lat = arcsin(clip(z)) uses z as is and clips /z/ > 1), points 1e-9 either side of the antimeridian, and 75 random unit directions. The image is 8x16 float32; output float64: rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.f32_dirs` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 14 | float32 DIRECTIONS (the current callers pass float64; float32 is what grid_dirs() returns by default): lon, lat and the fractional pixel position fx, fy are computed in float32 (resolution 4e-6 at fx = 32), while the weights mix with the int64 pixel index and the output is float64. 16x32 float32 noise image, 600 unit directions including the poles. The value is a continuous function of the direction, so a pixel-index flip near a texel edge costs nothing; measured against float64 directions: 1.5e-6, and 2.3e-6 with the directions nudged by one float32 ulp (poles and axes excluded: at a pole one ulp changes the longitude by any amount); the error grows with the width (4e-6 at 64 columns, 6.5e-6 at 128). Tolerance 3e-5 abs + 1e-5 rel (10x). |
| `sample_equirect.f64_image` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 10 | A float64 8x16 image sampled at 300 float64 unit directions (including the poles and the axes): the closed-form bilinear lookup with no float32 anywhere. rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.image_1x1` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 1 | Smallest images (1 rows x 1 columns), 40 unit directions: with one column both wrap indices are 0, with one row both clamped rows are row 0, so the result is the constant, the row or the column profile only. float64, rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.image_1x5` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 1 | Smallest images (1 rows x 5 columns), 40 unit directions: with one column both wrap indices are 0, with one row both clamped rows are row 0, so the result is the constant, the row or the column profile only. float64, rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.image_4x1` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 1 | Smallest images (4 rows x 1 columns), 40 unit directions: with one column both wrap indices are 0, with one row both clamped rows are row 0, so the result is the constant, the row or the column profile only. float64, rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.patch_rgb` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 12 | A (11, 11, 3) gnomonic patch of directions (lon pi, lat 80 degrees, half angle 14 degrees: it crosses the antimeridian AND the north pole) on a (16, 32, 3) float32 image: the leading shape of the directions is kept, the longitude wraps and the latitude clamps in the same call. float64 output, rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.texel_centers.2d` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 4 | The texel-center convention: sampling an 8x16 float32 image at the grid_dirs of its own size returns the image itself (column i is at longitude -pi + (i + 0.5) 2pi/W, row j at pi/2 - (j + 0.5) pi/H, so fx = i, fy = j exactly up to 1e-13). The output is float64 even for a float32 image. rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `sample_equirect.texel_centers.3d` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 8 | The same with a three-channel (8, 16, 3) image: the weights get a trailing axis, the output is (8, 16, 3) and equals the image. float64: rtol 1e-9. |
| `sample_equirect.texel_corners.2d` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 5 | Directions at the 9x17 CORNERS of the texels (half a texel off the centers): each sample is the mean of the four surrounding texels, with longitude wrapped (corner column 0 at -pi and column 16 at +pi both average the last and the first texel columns) and latitude clamped (corner row 0 at the pole averages row 0 with itself, row 8 row 7). The poles here are lat = +-pi/2 with x, y ~ 6e-17, not exact zeros. float64. |
| `sample_equirect.texel_corners.3d` | `cloudgen.sphere.sample_equirect` | 9 | The corner directions on the three-channel image: (9, 17, 3) output, the four-texel mean per channel (wrap in longitude, clamp in latitude). float64, rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `tangent_frame.f32_nonunit` | `cloudgen.sphere.tangent_frame` | 0 | float32 centers: np.cross promotes with the float64 z axis, so the frame is float64 computed from the float32 values (outputs float64). The last three centers are NOT unit length: east is still unit (it is normalized) but north = c x east then has the length of c (2, 5, 5 here). float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12. |
| `tangent_frame.generic` | `cloudgen.sphere.tangent_frame` | 2 | East = normalize(z x c), north = c x east, for 30 unit centers (the antimeridian at lon +-pi, mid and high latitudes, 20 random directions); the function takes one center and the case applies it to each row. float64 closed form: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12 (components that are 0 up to rounding, e.g. 1e-16 at the antimeridian). |
| `tangent_frame.poles_and_threshold` | `cloudgen.sphere.tangent_frame` | 1 | The pole fallback: when /z x c/ < 1e-9 the east vector is the fixed (0, 1, 0) and north = c x east (north pole: (-1, 0, 0); south pole: (+1, 0, 0)). Rows: exact poles, 1e-10, 3e-10 and 7.1e-10 from the axis (fallback taken), 3e-9, 2e-9, 1e-6 (normal branch, within a factor 3 of the threshold) and the equator. The exact threshold (/z x c/ == 1e-9) is not a case: it depends on how the norm is rounded. The threshold itself is pinned to 1e-4 (relative) by tangent_frame.threshold_bracket. float64, rtol 1e-9. |
| `tangent_frame.threshold_bracket` | `cloudgen.sphere.tangent_frame` | 1 | The pole guard /z x c/ < 1e-9 bracketed from both sides: 16 centers c = t * (-0.6, 0.8, 0) + (0, 0, +-1) with t in {0.9999, 1.0001, 0.999, 1.001, 0.99, 1.01, 0.9, 1.1} x 1e-9 (so /z x c/ = hypot(cx, cy) = t, exact inputs: the 3-4-5 direction keeps the norm free of rounding trouble up to 1e-16 relative). Rows with t < 1e-9 take the fixed fallback east = (0, 1, 0) (north = c x east = (-cz, 0, cx) = (-+1, 0, -0.6t)); rows with t > 1e-9 give the regular east = (-0.8, -0.6, 0), which differs from the fallback by O(1), so a threshold anywhere outside [0.9999e-9, 1.0001e-9] (that is +-1e-4 relative) flips at least one row (checked by mutation: 1.001e-9, 0.999e-9, 1.01e-9 and 0.99e-9 all fail). Every row is at least 9e-5 (relative) away from the threshold, far above the 1e-16 rounding of any way to compute the norm (hypot or sqrt of the sum of squares), and the exact threshold is not a case. float64: rtol 1e-9, atol 1e-12 (north components are ~1e-9). |
| `upsample_dirs.8x4_to_32x16` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 6 | A perturbed 8x4 grid of unit vectors (float32) upsampled to 32x16: the 3D vectors are interpolated bilinearly in float64 at the target texel centers (longitude wraps, rows above the first coarse row clamp) and renormalized, then rounded to float32. One band (rows < 4M / width). Output unit length to 4e-8. Measured: a float64 pipeline without the final rounding differs by 3e-8 (half a float32 ulp), a pipeline in float32 end to end (positions in float32) by 8e-7 at this size; tolerance 1e-5 abs + 1e-5 rel (10x the latter). At 4096 columns the all-float32 pipeline still differs by only 6.6e-6 (see the bands case). |
| `upsample_dirs.bands.4096x2048` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 456 | A 64x32 perturbed coarse grid upsampled to 4096x2048 (8.4M texels): the function works in bands of 4194304 // width = 1024 rows, so the target is processed in 2 bands; the result must not depend on the banding. The case stores 8 full rows (0, 1, 511, the last row of band 0 and the first and second row of band 1, and the last two rows) and the sum over the columns of EVERY row (3 components: a fingerprint of all 2048 rows) instead of the 100 MB map. Measured: the rows differ by 3e-8 from the float64 pipeline without its final rounding and by 6.6e-6 from a pipeline in float32 end to end; the row sums (each over 4096 components, up to 4000 in magnitude) by 3.2e-6 and 1.9e-3 respectively. Tolerances: rows 7e-5 abs + 1e-5 rel (10x the float32 pipeline), sums 2e-2 abs + 1e-5 rel (10x); a banding bug gives O(1) errors. |
| `upsample_dirs.from_1x1_to_4x2` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 0 | Smallest coarse grid, one vector: every target sample interpolates that vector with itself and the result is the vector renormalized, everywhere (shape (2, 4, 3)); spread 7e-9, same tolerance. |
| `upsample_dirs.identity.8x4` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 1 | Target size equal to the coarse size: the target texel centers are the coarse texel centers, so the result is the (already unit) input, normalized again. Checks the texel-center convention end to end (an off-by-half-texel bug would blur it). Measured spread 3e-8 (float64) / 3.6e-7 (all float32); tolerance as the other upsample cases. |
| `upsample_dirs.odd.5x3_to_11x7` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 1 | Odd sizes, 5x3 -> 11x7, FLOAT64 coarse input (the function converts to float64 either way; the output is float32 whatever the input dtype). Measured spread 3e-8 / 4.5e-7; same tolerance. |
| `upsample_dirs.tall_poles.16x512_to_8x2048` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 288 | A coarse 16x512 float32 grid (512 rows: the height of the production back-trace grid at structure_width 1024) upsampled to a tall, narrow 8x2048 target: the target grid MUST be built in float64 (grid_dirs(width, height, float64), as the function does). With a float32 target the latitude comes from arcsin of a z that is quantized at 6e-8, which is ill-conditioned in the rows just outside the pole clamp (d lat = d z / cos lat, and one coarse row is only 6e-3 rad): measured, the all-float32 target pipeline misses this output by 5.3e-04 and 6709 of 49152 components fall outside the tolerance, while a float64 pipeline without the final rounding to float32 differs by 3.0e-08 (half a float32 ulp). Tolerance 1e-5 relative + 1e-6 absolute (the golden.store default: at least 30x the float64 spread, and 300x for components near 1, and well below the float32-target error). The other upsample cases are too small to tell the two apart (their coarse grids have at most 16 rows). |
| `upsample_dirs.wide_target.16x8_to_64x4` | `cloudgen.sphere.upsample_dirs` | 4 | A target with FEWER rows than the coarse grid (16x8 -> 64x4: the north-south axis is decimated, the east-west axis upsampled): the function does not require an upsampling in both directions. Measured spread 3e-8 / 8.9e-7; same tolerance. |
| `worley3.f32.lattice` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__call__` | 25 | Points exactly ON cell corners (fractional part 0) up to +-5000: F1 is the distance from the corner to the nearest jittered feature of the 27-cell neighborhood. Exercises the exact-integer path of floor() and the cell indices past the 1024 wrap. Default tolerance. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `worley3.f32.random` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__call__` | 49 | F1 and F2 (distances to the nearest and second-nearest feature point, in cell units, one jittered point per unit cell, the 27 neighbor cells searched) at 1500 random float32 points in [-4, 4]^3 (cells of both signs: a negative cell index is wrapped by & 1023). float32 outputs, F1 <= F2, F1 < 1.08 and F2 < 1.2 over these points. Measured against the float64 evaluation of the same inputs: 1.3e-7 at most; the default tolerance (1e-5 rel, 1e-6 abs) is 10x that. The values are continuous in the point (min of distances), so there is no hard threshold even where the nearest cell changes. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `worley3.f32.wrap1024` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__call__` | 27 | The lattice repeats every 1024 cells (the index is & 1023, two's complement for negatives): 120 base points in cells 0..1023 with fractions k/64 (exact in float32), repeated shifted by multiples of 1024 (up to +-3 periods, both signs) and by -1024: F1 and F2 are bit-identical across the copies (asserted at build time). Default tolerance. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `worley3.f64_points` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__call__` | 45 | FLOAT64 points (800 in [-50, 50]^3): the cell index is floor() in float64, then the fractional part is rounded to float32, and the distances are float32: the outputs stay float32. The float32 kernel is the same as for float32 input; only the cell/fraction split differs. At this magnitude (float32 spacing 4e-6) a port that rounds the points to float32 BEFORE splitting still passes the default tolerance: worley3.f64_points.large is the case that pins the order. Default tolerance. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `worley3.f64_points.large` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__call__` | 45 | FLOAT64 points of large magnitude (800 in [-2000, 2000]^3, plus cells beyond the 1024 wrap and exact lattice points): floor() and the fraction x - floor(x) must be taken in FLOAT64 and only the fraction rounded to float32. Rounding the points to float32 first moves a coordinate of magnitude 1024..2048 by up to 6e-5, so F1 and F2 move by up to 8.0e-05 (measured), outside the default tolerance (1e-5 relative + 1e-6 absolute) for 646 (F1) and 568 (F2) of the 800 points (checked at build time). The float64-promoted evaluation of the same tables (exact fraction, float64 jitter and distances) differs by 1.3e-7 at most, so the default tolerance is at least 12x that. Shifting the points by a multiple of 1024 gives bit-identical outputs (the index wraps). A port that works in float32 throughout fails this case by design. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |
| `worley3.table` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__init__` | 36 | The constructor doubles the 1024-entry permutation to 2048 entries (int64) so that perm[perm[x] + y] with x, y < 1024 indexes up to 2046 without a wrap; the jitter table is 1024 x 3 float32 uniform draws in [0, 1) (both recorded, inputs). Output: the doubled permutation, exact. |
| `worley3.ties.centered_lattice` | `cloudgen.noise.Worley3.__call__` | 21 | Hand-made tables: identity permutation and every jitter 0.5, so the feature points form the lattice of cell centers and many distances tie exactly. At the center of a cell F1 = 0 and F2 = 1 (six neighbors tie at distance 1); on a cell face F1 = F2 = 0.5 (two features tie), at a corner F1 = F2 = sqrt(3)/2 (eight tie). A tie must give F2 == F1 (the second equal distance still counts as the second nearest). Tables are the inputs plan0.perm and plan0.jitter (seed null). Default tolerance. The permutation input plan<i>.perm is the draw as recorded (256 entries for Perlin3, 1024 for Worley3); the kernel indexes it DOUBLED, concat(perm, perm) (512 or 2048 entries, so p[i + 256] = p[i]): a port that indexes the stored array directly runs off its end (see the .table cases). |

## texture_surface

| case | function | KB | what it checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `at.bilinear.height_not_divisible` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 19 | A 64x30 surface (not 2:1) to width 32: 64 % 32 == 0 but 30 % 16 != 0, so the box branch is refused by its SECOND test and the bilinear branch runs, output 32x16. Default tolerance. |
| `at.bilinear_down.not_a_divisor` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 73 | 128x64 pattern to 48x24: 128 is not a multiple of 48, so the box branch is not taken and the surface is sampled bilinearly through sample_equirect at the texel-center directions of the 48x24 grid (longitude wraps at the antimeridian, latitude clamps at the poles; no pre-filtering). A float64 formula rounded to float32: default tolerance, measured worst difference against an analytic-index float64 evaluation 1.1e-7 (one float32 ulp). |
| `at.bilinear_up.x2` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 80 | 64x32 pattern up to 128x64 (w % width != 0, so bilinear): the first and the last output rows lie above the first and below the last source row center (latitude clamp: the pole row repeats), and column 0 and the last blend across the antimeridian. Default tolerance (as the other bilinear case). |
| `at.box_down.f2` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 80 | 128x64 pattern down to 64x32: width divides the surface width and half of it divides the height, so each output cell is the mean of a 2x2 block (float32 mean of float32 values, cast to float32). Default tolerance 1e-5/1e-6: measured worst difference against a float64 block mean 4.7e-8 (the float32 mean is a pairwise float32 sum). |
| `at.box_down.f4` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 68 | 128x64 pattern down to 32x16: width divides the surface width and half of it divides the height, so each output cell is the mean of a 4x4 block (float32 mean of float32 values, cast to float32). Default tolerance 1e-5/1e-6: measured worst difference against a float64 block mean 4.7e-8 (the float32 mean is a pairwise float32 sum). |
| `at.box_down.smallest_1x2` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 0 | The smallest result: an 8x4 pattern to width 2 (a 1x2 map): 8 % 2 == 0 and 4 % 1 == 0, a 4x4 block mean per cell. Default tolerance (measured as the other box cases). |
| `at.same_width.returns_itself` | `cloudgen.surface.Surface.at` | 32 | at(width) with width == the surface width returns the surface unchanged (no copy, no resampling): the outputs equal the inputs. Exact. |
| `continentality.all_land` | `cloudgen.surface.continentality` | 4 | An all-land field (1.0 everywhere, 32x16): the unit-sum kernel returns exactly 1.0 in float32 (the clip does not engage). Default tolerance. params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `continentality.all_sea` | `cloudgen.surface.continentality` | 4 | An all-sea field (0.0): exactly 0.0. params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `continentality.clip_bounds` | `cloudgen.surface.continentality` | 4 | A land field outside [0, 1] (1.5 in the northern half, -0.5 in the southern: not a real land fraction, chosen to engage both clips): the smoothing keeps 1.5 and -0.5 away from the boundary, and the clip returns exactly 1.0 and 0.0 there. A constant 1 or 0 field smooths to exactly itself, so only an out-of-range field shows the clip. Default tolerance (the clip makes the interior exact). params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `continentality.earth_synthetic128` | `cloudgen.surface.continentality` | 64 | The synthetic 128x64 land smoothed at CONTINENTALITY_KM = 300 and clipped to [0, 1], Earth radius (smoothing semantics: see smooth_on_sphere). Only the land field is read. Float32 chain: default tolerance (measured 5.8e-8 (a direct float64 convolution with no scipy and no float32 stage)). params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `continentality.moon_synthetic64` | `cloudgen.surface.continentality` | 16 | The same on a 64x32 land of a 1737 km body: 170 km texels make the 300 km smoothing 1.8 px, so the field is much sharper than on Earth. Default tolerance (measured 4.2e-8). params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `deck_strength.polar_cos_floor` | `cloudgen.surface.deck_strength` | 106 | Strength exp(-d / 1200 km) of marine stratocumulus for each ocean cell, d the distance to the next coast cell (cont >= 0.5, tested on the float32 values) DOWNSTREAM along its row (east on a prograde body; the row is mirrored for spin < 0, spin >= 0 counts as prograde), in wrapped columns times dx = 2 pi R / w max(cos lat, 1e-3); d x dx > 4000 km (strict) is infinite (strength 0); the result is multiplied by (1 - cont) (so a coast cell keeps at most 0.5). Rows with no coast or only coast are skipped (zeros). The exp is float64 rounded to float32, then a float32 product. `cont` is a float32 array and the test `cont >= 0.5` is made on those float32 values (0.5 is exact in both precisions): a port must keep cont in float32, because the hand-built inputs below put cells exactly on 0.5 and one float32 ulp either side of it. A tall 8-column x 1700-row field with random cont (sea and coast in most rows, 16 rows have none or only coast and are skipped): the half-texel pole rows have cos(lat) = 9.2e-4, floored to 1e-3 in dx (5.0 km instead of 4.6 km per column); every other row is above the floor. The case is sensitive to the floor (those two rows differ by 2e-3 without it). Default tolerance. params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |

The 12 rows of this 32-column field cover: a single coast cell (row 0), NO coast (row 1: zeros), ALL coast (row 2: zeros), a coast cell exactly 0.5 in column 0 (row 3), one just above 0.5 in the LAST column (row 4: the wrap), a run of coast and a cell at nextafter(0.5, 0) that is not coast (row 5), random, a full-land cell, scattered coasts, one coast in the last column, and a run to the last column. Radius 6371 km. spin = 0 equals +1. | `smooth_on_sphere.threshold_0_3px.tall_sweep` | `cloudgen.surface.smooth_on_sphere` | 125 | Semantics a port must reproduce (verified by an independent direct convolution with no scipy: it agrees to 5.8e-8 on every smoothing case): each row is filtered along longitude with its own sigma s = min(w/4, sigma_km / (px_km max(cos lat, 1e-3))) pixels, px_km = 2 pi R / w, only when s > 0.3 (otherwise the row is copied); then every column is filtered with sigma_km / px_km. scipy gaussian_filter1d: kernel taps -r..r with r = int(4 s + 0.5), weights exp(-0.5 x^2 / s^2) normalized to sum 1, accumulated in float64 and rounded to float32 once per pass; mode "wrap" is periodic (indices modulo w, even when r >= w), mode "nearest" repeats the edge row. THE ROW-FILTER THRESHOLD `s > 0.3`, pinned from both sides: an 8-column x 2000-row random field on Earth with sigma 1494.25 km, so the row sigma s = sigma / (px_km cos lat) is 0.2988 / cos(lat): it rises from 0.2988 px at the equator through 0.3 at /lat/ = 5.2 degrees to the quarter-width cap (2 px) at the poles, adjacent rows differing by about 1.4e-4 relative. Rows 939..1060 (the 122 rows with s <= 0.3, /lat/ < 5.2 degrees) are COPIED, the others filtered; 94 rows have s in (0.3, 0.303] and filter, the closest row to the threshold is 7.2e-05 (relative) away from it, so float64 rounding cannot move a row across. A threshold of 0.2995, 0.3005 or 0.303 (or 0.297) filters or copies rows that this field tells apart by about 4e-3 of their neighbors (the kernel weight of one pixel at sigma 0.3 is 3.9e-3). The column pass has sigma 0.299 px (radius 1). Default tolerance: measured worst difference against the float64 direct convolution 5.8e-8. params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `smooth_on_sphere.wide_sigma_caps` | `cloudgen.surface.smooth_on_sphere` | 16 | Semantics a port must reproduce (verified by an independent direct convolution with no scipy: it agrees to 5.8e-8 on every smoothing case): each row is filtered along longitude with its own sigma s = min(w/4, sigma_km / (px_km max(cos lat, 1e-3))) pixels, px_km = 2 pi R / w, only when s > 0.3 (otherwise the row is copied); then every column is filtered with sigma_km / px_km. scipy gaussian_filter1d: kernel taps -r..r with r = int(4 s + 0.5), weights exp(-0.5 x^2 / s^2) normalized to sum 1, accumulated in float64 and rounded to float32 once per pass; mode "wrap" is periodic (indices modulo w, even when r >= w), mode "nearest" repeats the edge row. The 64x32 synthetic land at 4000 km: row sigmas of 6.4 px / cos(lat), capped at w/4 = 16 px past /lat/ = 66 deg; the column sigma of 6.4 px on 32 rows is dominated by the "nearest" boundary. Default tolerance (measured 2.0e-8). params.constants lists the module constants and the inline numbers the function reads (a port copies them from its own source and may assert that they equal these). |
| `smoothstep.f32.cell_closed` | `cloudgen.texture._smoothstep` | 1 | smoothstep(a=0.03, b=0.30, x) on a float32 2-D array (the closed-cell edge ramp): a and b are weak python floats, so the arithmetic is float32 and the result float32. The sweep includes float32(a), float32(b), 0, -0 and +-1e30. Tolerance 1e-5/1e-6 (default): measured worst difference against the float64 evaluation of the same inputs 9e-8 (a chain of 6 float32 operations). |
| `smoothstep.f64.closed_ramp` | `cloudgen.texture._smoothstep` | 3 | Cubic smoothstep(a=0.4, b=0.7, x) in float64 over x = /lat/ / phi_H from 0 to 4 and a huge x. Closed form: float64 tolerance 1e-9 (measured: bit-identical to a math-module scalar evaluation on this machine (6e-17 at most for exp)). |
| `smoothstep.f64.ramp0_8` | `cloudgen.texture._smoothstep` | 3 | Cubic smoothstep(a=0, b=8, x) in float64, a sweep across both knees (x = a and x = b give exactly 0 and 1) plus 0, -0 and +-1e30 (the clip leaves no overflow). Closed form: float64 tolerance 1e-9; measured against math-module scalars: bit-identical to a math-module scalar evaluation on this machine (6e-17 at most for exp). |
| `standardize.constant_half` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 0 | A constant 0.5 float32 field (the float32 pairwise mean of 64 halves is exactly 0.5, so std is exactly 0): the same fallback gives 0. Exact. A port must keep sd == 0 as "divide by 1", not NaN. Only DYADIC constants (0, 0.5) are exact in float32 means: see the next case for the others. |
| `standardize.constant_non_dyadic.f32_n7` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 0 | NUMPY FLOAT32 ARTIFACT (a trap, not a model property): a CONSTANT float32 field of 7 values 0.1. The float32 mean (a sequential float32 sum of 7 values, then a float32 division by 7) is one ulp above 0.1f (7.45e-9), so v - mean = -7.45e-9 everywhere, the float32 std is 7.45e-9 > 0, the fallback is NOT taken and the result is -1 everywhere, not 0. The same constant with 8 or 16 values gives 0 (the unrolled pairwise sum is exact there), and with 64 values gives -1 again, so the outcome depends on the size and on float32 accumulation. A port that accumulates mean and std in float64 returns 0 for this input; a port that sums in float32, left to right, for n < 8 reproduces -1. The generator never standardizes a constant field, so a port may skip this case with that reason. Exact. |
| `standardize.constant_zero` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 0 | A constant field has std 0: the divisor falls back to 1.0 (the `sd > 0` branch), so the result is v - mean = 0, never NaN. Exact. See also constant_non_dyadic, where a float32 constant does NOT give std 0. |
| `standardize.f32.tiny_std_1e-15` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 0 | A float32 field with std 1.0e-15 (squares of 1e-30 stay normal float32, no denormal): still strictly positive, so the result has unit variance. A threshold of 1e-12 or 1e-9 instead of 0 would return values of order 1e-15. Default tolerance 1e-5/1e-6 (scale-free relative error, as the 1e-4 case). |
| `standardize.f32.tiny_std_1e-4` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 0 | A float32 field with std 1.0e-4 (mean ~0): small but strictly positive, so the divisor is the std itself and the result has unit variance. It pins the `sd > 0` test to ZERO: a threshold such as 1e-3 would fall back to dividing by 1 and return values of order 1e-4. Default tolerance 1e-5/1e-6: the same float32 statistics as the unit-interval case (measured worst difference 1.8e-7, relative error is scale-free). |
| `standardize.f32.unit_interval` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 4 | (v - mean) / std over a whole float32 2-D array with values in [0, 1]. The mean and std are float32 pairwise sums. Default tolerance 1e-5/1e-6: measured worst difference against float64 statistics 1.8e-7. |
| `standardize.f64.shifted` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 1 | The same on a float64 array (mean -7, std 3): float64 statistics, result float64. Tolerance 1e-9 (numpy sums pairwise, a plain loop differs by ~1e-16; measured against a direct float64 evaluation: 0). |
| `standardize.single_element` | `cloudgen.texture._standardize` | 0 | One element (the smallest legal array): its std is 0, the fallback applies, the result is 0. Exact. |
| `surface._gauss.convergence` | `cloudgen.surface._gauss` | 3 | surface._gauss(x, 0, 0.3) in float64 (the convergence-zone dip of land_contrast): even in x, 1 at 0, underflows to 0 at +-1e3. 1e-9 (measured against math.exp scalars: bit-identical to a math-module scalar evaluation on this machine (6e-17 at most for exp)). |
| `surface._gauss.desert` | `cloudgen.surface._gauss` | 3 | surface._gauss(x, 0.8, 0.25) in float64 (the subtropical-high dip). 1e-9. |
| `surface._smoothstep.fade_out` | `cloudgen.surface._smoothstep` | 3 | surface._smoothstep(2.2, 2.8, x) in float64 (the polar fade of land_contrast). Same sweep. 1e-9. |
| `surface._smoothstep.land_contrast_ramps` | `cloudgen.surface._smoothstep` | 3 | surface._smoothstep(0.35, 0.7, x) in float64 over x from -0.5 to 4, the knees and 1e9. Closed form: 1e-9 (measured: bit-identical to a math-module scalar evaluation on this machine (6e-17 at most for exp)). |

## tiles

| case | function | KB | what it checks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bake_tiles.defaults.no_args.stub_tile` | `bake_tiles.main` | 2 | NO flag at all: --size defaults to tiles.TILE_SIZE = 1024, --version to v2, --seed to 1, so the script asks build_tile for (1024, 1) and names its files cloud_cell_tiles_1024_v2. A real 1024 bake is too big for a case, so build_tile is replaced by a stand-in that returns the stored 8x8 tile (a fixed byte pattern, arange * 37 mod 256): the sidecar then says size 1024 over an 8x8 tile, which a real bake never does, and the only thing this case checks is the defaults (build_args, the file names and the sidecar fields) plus the encodings of an arbitrary tile. bake_tiles.py is a script: function names its main() (the encodings, not an importable core), run here as __main__ with a __file__ in a temporary folder (its output root) and sys.argv = [bake_tiles.py] + params.argv (the flags are --version, --seed and --size, with the defaults v2, 1 and TILE_SIZE = 1024) and the files it wrote decoded; their compressed bytes are not frozen. Input `tile`: the (size, size, 4) uint8 tile that build_tile returned (data here, not re-derived: raw_channels and build_tile have their own cases). Outputs: rgba = the gunzipped cloud_cell_tiles_<size>_<version>.rgba.gz, RGBA8 rows in order (row 0 first, the tile has no north), 4 bytes a texel in R heaps, G closed, B open, A sheet order = tile.reshape(-1); preview = the 8-bit grey PNG decoded, (4 size, 4 size): one band of `size` rows a channel in CHANNELS order, each band = [the channel rank / the coverage mask at 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.7, as 0 or 255]; sidecar_json = the .json the script writes, as canonical JSON bytes (parsed then re-dumped with sorted keys and separators , and :, ASCII; a port compares the parsed values, the script writes indent 2 and the platform line ending); files = the sorted paths written under the output root, one a line, with / separators (output/tiles/<version>/<stem>.rgba.gz, _preview.png and .json, stem = cloud_cell_tiles_<size>_<version>); build_args = (size, seed) as bake_tiles passed them to build_tile, int64 (2,). Pure byte layout and text: exact. |

Outputs: rgba = the gunzipped cloud_cell_tiles_<size>_<version>.rgba.gz, RGBA8 rows in order (row 0 first, the tile has no north), 4 bytes a texel in R heaps, G closed, B open, A sheet order = tile.reshape(-1); preview = the 8-bit grey PNG decoded, (4 size, 4 size): one band of `size` rows a channel in CHANNELS order, each band = [the channel rank / the coverage mask at 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.7, as 0 or 255]; sidecar_json = the .json the script writes, as canonical JSON bytes (parsed then re-dumped with sorted keys and separators , and :, ASCII; a port compares the parsed values, the script writes indent 2 and the platform line ending); files = the sorted paths written under the output root, one a line, with / separators (output/tiles/<version>/<stem>.rgba.gz, _preview.png and .json, stem = cloud_cell_tiles_<size>_<version>); build_args = (size, seed) as bake_tiles passed them to build_tile, int64 (2,). Pure byte layout and text: exact. |
| `build_tile.s32.seed2` | `cloudgen.tiles.build_tile` | 674 | The RGBA tile on 32x32, seed 2 (passed explicitly; params.defaults lists the defaults of the signature, size 1024 and seed 1, which no case can run at full size): stack(rank_bytes(raw_channels)) in CHANNELS order R heaps, G closed, B open, A sheet; every channel a rank (each byte level holds 4 texels of 1024, ties excepted: the open channel has a clear-center plateau of 19 equal texels). All random draws of the call are the inputs (plan0..plan19, the same 20 draws and order as raw_channels; 32x32 keeps the file small, the fixed-size lattices of the heaps and the Poisson sets are the same). params.plan_roles names the role of each draw (plan0 to plan2 the heaps lattices, plan3 to plan11 the closed channel, plan12 to plan18 the open channel, plan19 the sheet) and params.stages gives every formula with the inline literals that params.constants (the module constants) does not hold. NOTE: plan6 and plan7 are the SAME draw (the closed crack width is drawn with the seed of the y-warp fBm, seed + 105, low 3, slope 3: width and warp are one correlated field), and so are plan15 and plan16 in the open channel (seed + 155): a port that draws independent fields is statistically different from the Python maps; share the draw to match them. Bytes: exact (0 flipped texels in 8 nudge trials of every random input by one ulp and with scipy.fft). Output (32, 32, 4) uint8, row-major, channel last. |
| `cell_size.closed.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.cell_size_field` | 64 | Closed-cell size field (8 to 32 km, a factor 4 in size) on 64x64: values between 0.0334 and 0.1317 tile units (bounds 0.0333 and 0.1333), log-uniform and smooth. exp(log(km0/240) + (log(km1/240) - log(km0/240)) u), with u = _uniform(size, 1.0, seed) (slope 3); REFERENCE_TILE_KM = 240 is read from the source. The fBm white noise is the input plan0.white. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max relative /diff/ 1.1e-15 (5.3e-15 on the 5-decade case), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |

The fBm white noise is the input plan0.white. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max relative /diff/ 1.1e-15 (5.3e-15 on the 5-decade case), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |

The fBm white noise is the input plan0.white. | `cell_size.wide_range.s8` | `cloudgen.tiles.cell_size_field` | 1 | A huge dynamic range (1 km to 100 000 km: sizes from 0.0044 to 373 tile units, bounds 0.0042 and 417): the log/exp mapping over five decades. exp(log(km0/240) + (log(km1/240) - log(km0/240)) u), with u = _uniform(size, 1.0, seed) (slope 3); REFERENCE_TILE_KM = 240 is read from the source. | `coverage_masks.default.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.coverage_masks` | 16 | The deck's three coverages on a uniform 64x64 rank field, called as coverage_masks(rank) so the DEFAULT coverages (0.3, 0.5, 0.7) are the ones used (params.coverages lists them): masks rank >= round(256 (1 - c)) = 179, 128 and 77, so about 30, 50 and 70 percent of the texels. Output (3, 64, 64) bool, exact. |
| `coverage_masks.half_even_thresholds` | `cloudgen.tiles.coverage_masks` | 2 | The threshold int(round(256 (1 - c))) uses Python round, HALF TO EVEN, on a value that is exactly k + 0.5 here (c = 1 - (k + 0.5) / 256 is a dyadic float): 0.5 -> 0, 1.5 -> 2, 2.5 -> 2, 3.5 -> 4, 254.5 -> 254, 255.5 -> 256. On the ramp 0..255 the number of True texels is 256 - threshold, so the mask counts are 256, 254, 254, 252, 2, 0 (round-half-up would give 255, 254, 253, 252, 1 and 0). |
| `coverage_masks.out_of_range` | `cloudgen.tiles.coverage_masks` | 2 | Coverages outside (0, 1): 0.0 gives threshold 256 (nothing), 1.0 gives 0 and 1.5 gives -128 (everything), -0.5 gives 384 (nothing); numpy compares a uint8 array with such Python ints without wrapping. 0.3 -> 179. |
| `fbm.above_nyquist.s8` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 1 | low above every radial frequency (size 8 reaches 5.7): every amplitude is 0, the field is exactly 0 and the std guard max(std, 1e-12) keeps the division finite: the output is all 0, not NaN. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Output is exactly 0 in any implementation: exact. |
| `fbm.below_guard.low20.slope20.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 64 | THE STD GUARD, active: slope 20 gives a pre-normalization std of 2.0e-14, BELOW 1e-12, so the division is by the guard, not by the std: the output is (field - mean) / 1e-12 and its std is 0.020, not 1. This is the case that pins the guard VALUE from below: the slope 10 and 14 cases bound it from above (it must stay under 1.8e-10), this one fixes it (the output scales as 1/guard: a guard of 1e-15 gives std 1 here, 1e-9 gives 2e-5). Never reached by the generator. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: max /diff/ 2.8e-17 on values up to 0.075 (1.5e-14 relative above 1e-3; scipy.fft 2.1e-17), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-12 absolute: the guard is pinned to better than 1e-9 relative. |
| `fbm.even_nonpow2.s12` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 2 | An even non-power-of-two size (12 = 4 x 3): the Nyquist row is index 6 (k = -6), and the transform is a mixed-radix one. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |
| `fbm.fftfreq_inexact.s14` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 3 | NUMPY ARTIFACT: fftfreq(14) * 14 gives 4.999999999999999 for k = 5 (it is computed as (i * (1/14)) * 14, not an exact integer), so the modes (+-5, 0) and (0, +-5) have radius just BELOW low = 5.0 and are dropped, where an exact-integer k would keep them. Power-of-two sizes (every size the product uses) are exact and unaffected; a port that wants this case computes k as (i as f64 * (1.0 / n as f64)) * n as f64, i in 0..n/2 then -n/2..-1. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: the spread under nudges is the same as the other fBm cases (max /diff/ 1e-15); the artifact itself moves the output by 0.11 (max /diff/; 123 of 196 modes are kept, 127 with an exact k), so the case does tell the two apart. |

Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |

The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |
| `fbm.slope0.low0.s16` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 4 | slope 0 and low 0: the amplitude is 1 for every mode INCLUDING the DC mode (radius 0 passes `kk >= 0`, and max(0, 1e-9)^-0 = 1), so the field is the white noise itself, normalized: (white - mean) / std. Well-conditioned way to hit the max(kk, 1e-9) guard. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |
| `fbm.slope_negative.low0.s16` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 4 | slope -2 and low 0: a rising spectrum (amplitude = radius), and the DC mode gets the guarded radius 1e-9 instead of 0 (amplitude 1e-9, removed again by the mean). Exercises the negative exponent and the DC guard together. low <= 0 with a positive slope is NOT a case: the DC amplitude 3e13 makes the result numerically meaningless (error 8e-3). The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |

The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Exactly 0: exact. |
| `fbm.texture.slope2.s32` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 16 | The closed-cell texture setting (low 12, slope 2) on a 32x32 tile: a flatter spectrum, highest radial frequency 22.6, more than half of the modes pass the low cut. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |
| `fbm.tiny_std.low20.slope10.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 64 | THE STD GUARD, above it: low 20 and slope 10 on 64x64 keep only the modes of radius 20 to 45 with an amplitude radius^-5 (3e-7 down to 5e-9), so the field BEFORE normalization has a population std of 8.9e-8 (tiny but not zero). The guard max(std, 1e-12) must not touch it: the output has std exactly 1 (a guard of 1e-6 would give 0.09, one of 1e-7 would give 0.9). Never reached by the shipped parameters (low <= 12, slope <= 3); it pins the guard as a floor at 1e-12 and nothing larger. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (4.4e-13 relative on values above 1e-3; scipy.fft 1.3e-15): the filter is applied to the spectrum, so the tiny amplitude costs no relative accuracy. 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |
| `fbm.tiny_std.low20.slope14.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 64 | THE STD GUARD, further above it: slope 14 gives a pre-normalization std of 1.8e-10, still about 180 times the guard: the output has std 1 (a guard of 1e-9 or more would shrink it to 0.18 or less). Same setting as the slope 10 case with a steeper spectrum. The white noise the seed draws is the input plan0.white (size, size). FFT convention: numpy fft2 unnormalized forward, ifft2 divides by size^2, k = fftfreq(size) * size. Measured: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6.4e-13 relative above 1e-3; scipy.fft 1.8e-15): 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |

Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 1.8e-15 (6e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute. |
| `inverted.clamp_at_one_cell` | `cloudgen.tiles._inverted` | 1 | max(0, 1 - f1): 1 at a feature point, 0 a full cell away and beyond (distances above 1, 1e9 and infinity clamp to 0), and a negative distance (-0.25) gives 1.25 (no upper clamp). One float64 subtraction and a max are correctly rounded, so the result is bit-exact in any IEEE port: exact. |
| `lumps.fifth_nearest_ignored.s64.seed828` | `cloudgen.tiles.lumps` | 116 | ONLY THE 4 NEAREST ARE QUERIED. On this draw the texel (row 6, col 47) is covered by a 5th-nearest lump whose dome is 0.066 higher than the best of the 4 nearest: Python IGNORES it (k = 4), so a port that scans every lump covering a texel (a 3x3 cell neighborhood, k = 5 or more) differs there and nowhere else on this tile (about 2 texels in 4 million do). The texel (row 37, col 10) is won by the 4th-nearest lump (a max over 3 is lower by 0.115). The jittered lump grid (60 x 60 = 3600 centers g, [x, y] in tile units, and their radii r (0.35 to 0.7 of the spacing)) is the input (plan0.g, plan0.r); freq = round(240 / 4) = 60 is read from the source (params.constants). dome = max over the 4 NEAREST lumps (by distance, periodic KD tree, not by normalized distance) of clip(1 - dist / r, 0, 1)^0.6. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 6e-14, because x^0.6 has an infinite slope at 0 (a distance within an ulp of r moves the value by up to (1e-16)^0.6 = 1.6e-10): 1e-9 relative, 1e-9 absolute. |
| `lumps.fourth_nearest_and_peak.s64.seed732` | `cloudgen.tiles.lumps` | 116 | THE 4TH-NEAREST LUMP AND THE DOME PEAK. On this draw the dome at texels (row 23, col 6) and (row 60, col 25) comes from the FOURTH-nearest lump (a lower radius loses to a farther, wider one): a max over the 3 nearest would be lower there by 5.6e-04 and 2.0e-02. The texel (row 16, col 37) lies at dist / r = 0.0012 of a lump center, where the dome is 0.99925: the upper bound of clip(1 - dist / r, 0, 1) never binds (dist >= 0), so the dome rises to 1 at a center with no flat top; a port that caps it below 1, say at 0.99, loses 6e-3 here. Both facts are invisible on a typical draw (lumps.s64), hence the searched seed. The jittered lump grid (60 x 60 = 3600 centers g, [x, y] in tile units, and their radii r (0.35 to 0.7 of the spacing)) is the input (plan0.g, plan0.r); freq = round(240 / 4) = 60 is read from the source (params.constants). dome = max over the 4 NEAREST lumps (by distance, periodic KD tree, not by normalized distance) of clip(1 - dist / r, 0, 1)^0.6. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 6e-14, because x^0.6 has an infinite slope at 0 (a distance within an ulp of r moves the value by up to (1e-16)^0.6 = 1.6e-10): 1e-9 relative, 1e-9 absolute. |

The jittered lump grid (60 x 60 = 3600 centers g, [x, y] in tile units, and their radii r (0.35 to 0.7 of the spacing)) is the input (plan0.g, plan0.r); freq = round(240 / 4) = 60 is read from the source (params.constants). dome = max over the 4 NEAREST lumps (by distance, periodic KD tree, not by normalized distance) of clip(1 - dist / r, 0, 1)^0.6. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 6e-14, because x^0.6 has an infinite slope at 0 (a distance within an ulp of r moves the value by up to (1e-16)^0.6 = 1.6e-10): 1e-9 relative, 1e-9 absolute. |
| `ndtr.grid` | `scipy.special.ndtr` | 5 | The normal CDF that _uniform applies to a unit fBm: x from -8.5 to 8.5, and far tails down to -37 (5.7e-300) where 1 - 0.5 erfc(x / sqrt 2) would lose everything: a port must use the erfc form for negative x. Only the main range /x/ < 5 matters to the tiles (the fBm is unit variance); the tails pin the erfc. scipy returns an accurate-to-the-ulp value, so 1e-9 relative with an absolute floor of 1e-300 (the tail is compared relatively). |
| `poisson.constant_field.s4` | `cloudgen.tiles.poisson_points` | 5 | A constant size field 0.25: s_min / s = 1 so every candidate is kept (192 = int(12 / 0.25^2)) and the dart throwing alone decides: a plain Poisson disc of radius 0.2125 (13 points; 16 without the wrap). The candidates and the thinning mask the seed draws are the inputs plan0.cand (n, 2: x, y) and plan0.keep (n,); n = int(12.0 / min(size_field)^2) and the thinning is keep = (u < (min size / size at the candidate)^2), the size being the nearest-texel one. The uniforms u of the thinning draw are the input thin_u (n,) (the code's own plan keeps only their outcome, so the case module records them): a port that draws its own cand and u derives keep from them, and the output keep_from_u (bool, n, exact: the square is one multiplication r * r, the comparison STRICT) is what it must obtain; it equals plan0.keep. The dart throwing over cand[keep] (scan order, a candidate is rejected when a PERIODIC distance to any accepted point is < spacing * max(its size, the accepted point's size), STRICTLY) is sequential and order-dependent, so the accepted points are an exact output, with their indices into cand[keep]. Accepted points are copies of candidates (exact); the accept decisions flip only for a distance within an ulp of its threshold, which never occurred in 8 trials with every candidate and size nudged by one ulp. |
| `poisson.exact_tie.injected.s1` | `cloudgen.tiles.poisson_points` | 1 | EXACT TIE, with the candidates INJECTED by the case module (no seed puts a distance on a threshold to the ulp; the arrays stored are the plan0.cand / plan0.keep any case stores, the seed argument is unused): a 1x1 field of 0.5 gives 48 = int(12 / 0.25) candidates, spacing 1.0 makes the rejection threshold exactly 0.5, all kept. The candidates alternate (0.125, 0.25) and (0.625, 0.25): their distance is exactly 0.5 on both sides of the torus (hypot(0.5, 0) = 0.5), so `0.5 < 0.5` is false and the second point is ACCEPTED; the 46 later candidates are exact copies of those two (distance 0) and are rejected. Two points come out; a port that rejects at `<=` accepts one. The candidates and the thinning mask the seed draws are the inputs plan0.cand (n, 2: x, y) and plan0.keep (n,); n = int(12.0 / min(size_field)^2) and the thinning is keep = (u < (min size / size at the candidate)^2), the size being the nearest-texel one. The uniforms u of the thinning draw are the input thin_u (n,) (the code's own plan keeps only their outcome, so the case module records them): a port that draws its own cand and u derives keep from them, and the output keep_from_u (bool, n, exact: the square is one multiplication r * r, the comparison STRICT) is what it must obtain; it equals plan0.keep. The dart throwing over cand[keep] (scan order, a candidate is rejected when a PERIODIC distance to any accepted point is < spacing * max(its size, the accepted point's size), STRICTLY) is sequential and order-dependent, so the accepted points are an exact output, with their indices into cand[keep]. Accepted points are copies of candidates (exact); in THIS injected case the decisions sit exactly on a threshold by construction, which is why the result is exact (the inputs are copied, not recomputed) and why a port must compare as stated. |
| `poisson.keep_tie.injected.s2` | `cloudgen.tiles.poisson_points` | 5 | THE THINNING COMPARISON, with the draws INJECTED by the case module (the seed argument is unused): a 2x2 size field [[0.25, 0.5], [0.5, 0.5]] gives 192 = int(12 / 0.0625) candidates and keep ratio 1 in the 0.25 texel, (0.25 / 0.5)^2 = 0.25 EXACTLY in the others. Candidate 0 (0.25, 0.25), u = 0.5: kept. Candidate 1 (0.75, 0.75), u = 0.25 = its ratio: NOT kept (`u < ratio` is strict; `<=` would keep it and accept a third point). Candidate 2 (0.75, 0.25), u = the float just BELOW 0.25: kept (no epsilon: the comparison is exact). Candidate 3 (0.25, 0.75), u = the float just ABOVE 0.25: not kept. The 188 later candidates repeat candidates 1 and 3 and are all not kept. Two points come out, candidate 0 and 2; keep_from_u pins the thinning of a port that draws its own uniforms. Spacing 0.85 (default): the accepted points are 0.5 apart and the threshold 0.85 max(0.5, 0.25) = 0.425. The candidates and the thinning mask the seed draws are the inputs plan0.cand (n, 2: x, y) and plan0.keep (n,); n = int(12.0 / min(size_field)^2) and the thinning is keep = (u < (min size / size at the candidate)^2), the size being the nearest-texel one. The uniforms u of the thinning draw are the input thin_u (n,) (the code's own plan keeps only their outcome, so the case module records them): a port that draws its own cand and u derives keep from them, and the output keep_from_u (bool, n, exact: the square is one multiplication r * r, the comparison STRICT) is what it must obtain; it equals plan0.keep. The dart throwing over cand[keep] (scan order, a candidate is rejected when a PERIODIC distance to any accepted point is < spacing * max(its size, the accepted point's size), STRICTLY) is sequential and order-dependent, so the accepted points are an exact output, with their indices into cand[keep]. Accepted points are copies of candidates (exact); in THIS injected case the decisions sit exactly on a threshold by construction, which is why the result is exact (the inputs are copied, not recomputed) and why a port must compare as stated. |
| `poisson.no_candidates.s1` | `cloudgen.tiles.poisson_points` | 0 | A 1x1 field of 4.0 gives int(12 / 16) = 0 candidates: empty (0, 2) arrays in, an empty (0, 2) point set and an empty index out (zero-length arrays are legal in the store: offset and nbytes are 0). The candidates and the thinning mask the seed draws are the inputs plan0.cand (n, 2: x, y) and plan0.keep (n,); n = int(12.0 / min(size_field)^2) and the thinning is keep = (u < (min size / size at the candidate)^2), the size being the nearest-texel one. The uniforms u of the thinning draw are the input thin_u (n,) (the code's own plan keeps only their outcome, so the case module records them): a port that draws its own cand and u derives keep from them, and the output keep_from_u (bool, n, exact: the square is one multiplication r * r, the comparison STRICT) is what it must obtain; it equals plan0.keep. The dart throwing over cand[keep] (scan order, a candidate is rejected when a PERIODIC distance to any accepted point is < spacing * max(its size, the accepted point's size), STRICTLY) is sequential and order-dependent, so the accepted points are an exact output, with their indices into cand[keep]. Accepted points are copies of candidates (exact); the accept decisions flip only for a distance within an ulp of its threshold, which never occurred in 8 trials with every candidate and size nudged by one ulp. |
