| `rank_bytes.distinct.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 36 | A 64x64 field of distinct values: the rank histogram is exactly uniform, 16 texels per byte level, and the byte is a monotone function of the value. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.extremes.nonsquare.5x7` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 0 | A non-square 5x7 field with -inf, +inf, -1e300, 1e300, and ties: -0.0 and two +0.0 are EQUAL (np.unique groups them: one mean rank shared, a total-order compare would split them) and two -3.5. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.few_levels.15x17` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 2 | Five distinct values (1, 7, 100, 30 and 117 texels, scattered) on 15x17 = 255 texels: mean ranks 0, 4, 57.5, 122.5 and 196 (half-integers for the even-sized groups), bytes 0, 4, 57, 122, 196 = floor(256 mean / 255). 1.0 and the next float64 above it are DIFFERENT values (exact comparison, no epsilon). Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.float32_ties.s32` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 5 | A FLOAT32 field quantized to 41 levels (many ties): np.unique on float32 values, the same mean-rank arithmetic in float64. The tiles never pass float32; the case pins that ties are decided in the field's own dtype. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.n196.s14` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 2 | n = 196 distinct values: here rank * (256 / 196) is off by one from floor(rank * 256 / 196) at 3 ranks (float rounding of the pre-divided factor). A port must multiply first (rank * 256) then divide, or use integer arithmetic floor(256 rank / n); the case fails the other order. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.open_channel.plateau.s32` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 9 | The real open channel of raw_channels(32, seed 2): float64, about 2% of the texels are exact zeros (clear centers) that must share one byte, the rest distinct. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.plateau.quarter_zero.s32` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 9 | A quarter of the field (the 16x16 corner) is exactly 0 (an open cell's empty center): the 256 equal texels take ONE mean rank, hence one byte, never a ramp down the rows. The plateau is the lowest value, mean rank 127.5 of 1024 texels: byte 31. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.scan_order.column_major_ramp.s32` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 9 | Values increase DOWN the columns first (value = col * 32 + row): the bytes are rank // 4 in C scan order of the flattened field, so a transposed port shows at once. Output shape equals the input shape. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.single_texel.s1` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 0 | The smallest legal field, 1x1: one value, rank 0, byte 0. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.tiny.3x3` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 0 | n = 9 < 256: the bytes are sparse (0, 28, 56, 85, 113, 142, 170, 199, 227) and the top byte is NOT 255. Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `rank_bytes.uniform_field.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.rank_bytes` | 36 | Every texel equal: one group of n = 4096, mean rank (n - 1) / 2 = 2047.5, byte floor(2047.5 * 256 / 4096) = 127 everywhere (not 0, not 128). Ranks are integers in float64 arithmetic: byte = floor(rank * 256 / n) with the MEAN rank of equal values; byte output: exact. |
| `raw_channels.s48.seed1` | `cloudgen.tiles.raw_channels` | 868 | The four fields before rank encoding on a 48x48 tile, called as raw_channels(48) so the default seed 1 is the one used (params.defaults lists the defaults of the signature: size 1024, seed 1): heaps (three Worley lattices 8, 32 and 96 cells under an envelope), closed (cracks of a warped Voronoi with a wandering half-width, closing where open_crack drops to 0, domes, lumpy tops on a 64-cell lattice), open (a wall band times the lumps plus the faint wall and inward terms, with a clear-center plateau of 66 exact zeros) and sheet (an fBm, low 2, slope 3). ALL the random draws of the call are the inputs, plan0 to plan19 in call order (worley, fbm, poisson, lumps; the descriptors in params.plans give the size, freq, seed... of each draw). params.plan_roles names the role of each draw (plan0 to plan2 the heaps lattices, plan3 to plan11 the closed channel, plan12 to plan18 the open channel, plan19 the sheet) and params.stages gives every formula with the inline literals that params.constants (the module constants) does not hold. NOTE: plan6 and plan7 are the SAME draw (the closed crack width is drawn with the seed of the y-warp fBm, seed + 105, low 3, slope 3: width and warp are one correlated field), and so are plan15 and plan16 in the open channel (seed + 155): a port that draws independent fields is statistically different from the Python maps; share the draw to match them. The per-function cases cover every piece, this one the composition and the call order. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 5.6e-14 (closed), 2.2e-15 to 2.2e-14 on the others (scipy.fft: 7.3e-15), no flipped Voronoi cell and no flipped rank byte: 1e-9 relative / 1e-9 absolute. Intermediates (width, open_crack, depth, sag, tops, texture, wall...) are not frozen: each is a per-function case or a closed-form expression of them (params.stages). |
| `sample.nearest_texel.m22` | `cloudgen.tiles._sample` | 6 | Nearest-texel lookup field[trunc(y m) % m, trunc(x m) % m] on a 22x22 field whose value is row * 22 + col (so the output names both indices): points at k/22 (15/22 * 22 = 14.999999999999998 truncates to 14), exactly 1.0 and just below (wrap to 0 / last texel), NEGATIVE points (astype(int64) truncates toward zero, so -1e-9 and -0.03 give index 0, not 21; -0.07 gives -1 % 22 = 21), points beyond one tile (1.5, 2.25, 1e9) and random points. The field is SQUARE: _sample is only defined for square fields (m = shape[0] indexes both axes, see sample.non_square.rows_index_both_axes.5x9). Pure selection of a float64 array: exact. |
| `sample.non_square.rows_index_both_axes.5x9` | `cloudgen.tiles._sample` | 1 | OUTSIDE THE CONTRACT, pinned so that nobody generalizes it silently: _sample is defined for SQUARE fields (every tile, size field and map of tiles.py is square). m = shape[0] is used for BOTH axes, so on a 5x9 field (value row * 9 + col) the column is trunc(x * 5) % 5 and columns 5 to 8 are never read. A port that scales the column by shape[1] differs here and nowhere else. Exact. |
| `sample.single_texel.m1` | `cloudgen.tiles._sample` | 0 | A 1x1 field: every index is trunc(p) % 1 = 0, whatever the point: the smallest legal tile. Exact. |
| `smoothstep.equal_edges` | `cloudgen.tiles._smoothstep` | 0 | Degenerate a == b: (x - a) / 0 is -inf, NaN (0/0) or +inf, which np.clip turns into 0, NaN and 1. Never reached by the generator (every call has a < b); pins the IEEE division semantics. |
| `smoothstep.float32` | `cloudgen.tiles._smoothstep` | 1 | The same function on a float32 array: the Python float edges stay weak, so the whole chain is float32 and the result float32. The tiles code never passes float32; the case pins the dtype rule. Tolerance 1e-5 relative / 1e-6 absolute (float32 kernel of a few operations; the same computation in float64 differs by 1.4e-7 absolute, 4.6e-7 relative). |
| `smoothstep.nonfinite` | `cloudgen.tiles._smoothstep` | 0 | _smoothstep with infinities and a NaN: np.clip maps +-inf to the clip bounds and PROPAGATES the NaN (a max/min based clamp would turn it into 0 or 1). Never reached by the generator; the case fixes the numpy semantics. NaN compares equal to NaN in the check. |
| `smoothstep.reversed_edges` | `cloudgen.tiles._smoothstep` | 1 | Reversed edges a > b: the denominator is negative, the ramp falls from 1 to 0 (the clip bounds swap sides). Never used by the generator; closed form, 1e-9 relative. |
| `smoothstep.unit_0_1` | `cloudgen.tiles._smoothstep` | 5 | _smoothstep(0, 1, x) (the border/width ramp of the closed cracks and the sag): x from -1 to 2, so both clips and the identity-denominator ramp are taken. Float64 closed form: 1e-9 relative. |
| `smoothstep.window_0p25_0p65` | `cloudgen.tiles._smoothstep` | 4 | _smoothstep(0.25, 0.65, x) (the open-crack window of raw_channels) over a grid that sits below the window (clipped to 0), inside it (the cubic k^2 (3 - 2k)) and above it (clipped to 1), plus x exactly at both edges and +-1e300. A float64 closed form of a few operations: 1e-9 relative, 1e-12 absolute. |
| `stat.fbm.sheet.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.periodic_fbm` | 2 | The sheet fBm (low 2, slope 3) on 64x64: mean 0 and std 1 by construction, so the metrics are shape ones. STATISTICAL: 40 seeds (1000..1039), 5 metrics (names and definitions in params). The port draws its own random numbers, so it cannot reproduce `values`; it computes each metric exactly as defined, for its own seeds, and checks /metric - mean/ <= n_sigma * std per metric for one seed (n_sigma * std / sqrt(m) for the mean of m seeds). mean and std (ddof = 1) are what the Python code produces; the recomputation check holds them at 1e-9 (no flipped texel in the nudge experiments). |
| `stat.lumps.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.lumps` | 1 | Cumulus lumps on 64x64 (60x60 jittered centers, radii 0.35 to 0.7 of the spacing). | `texel_centers.s5` | `cloudgen.tiles._texel_centers` | 0 | Texel centers ((i + 0.5) / 5) of a 5x5 tile as (size^2, 2) rows [x, y] in row-major order (x = column varies fastest). One correctly rounded float64 division per coordinate: bit-identical in any IEEE port, checked at 1e-12 relative. |

| `uniform.default_slope.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles._uniform` | 64 | ndtr of a unit periodic fBm (low 1: the cell-size field source), default slope. Slope 3.0. The fBm white noise is the input plan0.white. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 6.7e-16, so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute (ndtr is a contraction: its slope is at most 0.4). |
| `uniform.slope2.low12.s32` | `cloudgen.tiles._uniform` | 16 | The same with the closed-cell texture settings (low 12, slope 2 passed explicitly). Slope 2.0. The fBm white noise is the input plan0.white. Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 6.7e-16, so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute (ndtr is a contraction: its slope is at most 0.4). |
| `warped_voronoi.constant_field.s16` | `cloudgen.tiles.warped_voronoi` | 12 | A constant size field 0.3 on 16x16 (9 cells): a small point set, so the periodic KD tree has few points and a query reaches its images across the tile edges often; s_cell is the constant 0.3. Draws in call order: plan0 = the Poisson candidates and keep mask of poisson_points(size_field, seed) (default spacing 0.85; the thinning that produced the keep mask from uniforms is pinned by the poisson.* cases, input thin_u), plan1 and plan2 = the white noise of the two warp fBms (low 3, slope 3, seeds seed + 1 and seed + 2); the warp is BORDER_WARP (0.12) x the local size x the fBm, added to the texel center and wrapped with np.mod(., 1.0); the 2 nearest points come from a periodic KD tree (boxsize 1). Outputs: border = (F2 - F1) / size of the nearest cell; s_cell = the size of the nearest cell, a COPY of a size_field value (a port that is handed size_field copies it: bit-exact; it is compared at 1e-12 relative only because the Python check recomputes size_field with the local FFT, which may differ by an ulp from the frozen one); and the accepted Poisson points, an intermediate (exact: copies of the drawn candidates). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: border max /diff/ 8.6e-15 (F2 - F1 cancels), s_cell unchanged in every trial (the nearest cell never flipped), so border rtol 1e-9 / atol 1e-10, s_cell rtol 1e-12. |
| `warped_voronoi.open.s64` | `cloudgen.tiles.warped_voronoi` | 241 | The variable Voronoi cells of the open regime on 64x64: Poisson points, a warped query, the periodic k=2 query, the border value (0 on a border, about 0.5 to 1 at a center). Draws in call order: plan0 = the Poisson candidates and keep mask of poisson_points(size_field, seed) (default spacing 0.85; the thinning that produced the keep mask from uniforms is pinned by the poisson.* cases, input thin_u), plan1 and plan2 = the white noise of the two warp fBms (low 3, slope 3, seeds seed + 1 and seed + 2); the warp is BORDER_WARP (0.12) x the local size x the fBm, added to the texel center and wrapped with np.mod(., 1.0); the 2 nearest points come from a periodic KD tree (boxsize 1). Outputs: border = (F2 - F1) / size of the nearest cell; s_cell = the size of the nearest cell, a COPY of a size_field value (a port that is handed size_field copies it: bit-exact; it is compared at 1e-12 relative only because the Python check recomputes size_field with the local FFT, which may differ by an ulp from the frozen one); and the accepted Poisson points, an intermediate (exact: copies of the drawn candidates). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: border max /diff/ 8.6e-15 (F2 - F1 cancels), s_cell unchanged in every trial (the nearest cell never flipped), so border rtol 1e-9 / atol 1e-10, s_cell rtol 1e-12. |
| `warped_voronoi.single_point.s4` | `cloudgen.tiles.warped_voronoi` | 1 | DEGENERATE: a size field of 2.0 (a cell larger than the tile) accepts exactly one point, so the k=2 query has no second neighbor: scipy returns distance inf (and index n), border = inf everywhere and s_cell is that point's size. Never reached by the generator (sizes are at most 0.2); pins the cKDTree k > n semantics (inf, not an error). The output holds infinities (equal to themselves in the check). Draws in call order: plan0 = the Poisson candidates and keep mask of poisson_points(size_field, seed) (default spacing 0.85; the thinning that produced the keep mask from uniforms is pinned by the poisson.* cases, input thin_u), plan1 and plan2 = the white noise of the two warp fBms (low 3, slope 3, seeds seed + 1 and seed + 2); the warp is BORDER_WARP (0.12) x the local size x the fBm, added to the texel center and wrapped with np.mod(., 1.0); the 2 nearest points come from a periodic KD tree (boxsize 1). Outputs: border = (F2 - F1) / size of the nearest cell; s_cell = the size of the nearest cell, a COPY of a size_field value (a port that is handed size_field copies it: bit-exact; it is compared at 1e-12 relative only because the Python check recomputes size_field with the local FFT, which may differ by an ulp from the frozen one); and the accepted Poisson points, an intermediate (exact: copies of the drawn candidates). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: border max /diff/ 8.6e-15 (F2 - F1 cancels), s_cell unchanged in every trial (the nearest cell never flipped), so border rtol 1e-9 / atol 1e-10, s_cell rtol 1e-12. |
| `warped_voronoi.zero_warp.s4` | `cloudgen.tiles.warped_voronoi` | 6 | size 4: the two warp fBms have low 3 above the highest radius of a 4x4 tile (2.83), so both are the all-zero field and the query is the texel center itself (the std-guard path of periodic_fbm inside warped_voronoi); 0.2 gives int(12 / 0.04000000000000001) = int(299.99999999999994) = 299 candidates (the truncation matters) and 19 points. Draws in call order: plan0 = the Poisson candidates and keep mask of poisson_points(size_field, seed) (default spacing 0.85; the thinning that produced the keep mask from uniforms is pinned by the poisson.* cases, input thin_u), plan1 and plan2 = the white noise of the two warp fBms (low 3, slope 3, seeds seed + 1 and seed + 2); the warp is BORDER_WARP (0.12) x the local size x the fBm, added to the texel center and wrapped with np.mod(., 1.0); the 2 nearest points come from a periodic KD tree (boxsize 1). Outputs: border = (F2 - F1) / size of the nearest cell; s_cell = the size of the nearest cell, a COPY of a size_field value (a port that is handed size_field copies it: bit-exact; it is compared at 1e-12 relative only because the Python check recomputes size_field with the local FFT, which may differ by an ulp from the frozen one); and the accepted Poisson points, an intermediate (exact: copies of the drawn candidates). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: border max /diff/ 8.6e-15 (F2 - F1 cancels), s_cell unchanged in every trial (the nearest cell never flipped), so border rtol 1e-9 / atol 1e-10, s_cell rtol 1e-12. |
| `worley.dense.s4.f16` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 4 | More cells than texels (freq 16 on 4 texels): every texel center lands exactly on a cell edge (x = 4i + 2 is an integer): floor and the neighbor window must still find F1/F2 (the 3x3 window around the floor cell covers the true neighbors). The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
| `worley.odd_ratio.s10.f3` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 2 | size 10 on 3 cells: texel centers fall at non-dyadic fractions of a cell (0.3 (i + 0.5)), never on a cell edge, and the periodic jitter lookup has 3 distinct columns. The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
| `worley.s128.f16` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 260 | A 128x128 tile at 16 cells (8 texels a cell): the larger tile size. The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
| `worley.s64.f32` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 80 | The same at 32 cells on 64 texels (the heaps envelope frequency, two texels a cell): a denser lattice with more neighbor changes per row. The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
| `worley.s64.f8` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 65 | F1/F2 distances (in cell units) to a jittered 8x8 grid that wraps on a 64x64 tile, at texel centers: the 3x3 neighborhood search, the wrapped jitter lookup (index % freq) and the unwrapped distance. The center coordinate is ((i + 0.5) / size) * freq, in that order. The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
| `worley.single_cell.f1` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 4 | freq = 1: all nine neighbors read the same jitter (index % 1 = 0), so F1 is the distance to the one point and F2 the distance to its nearest periodic image: the wrap with the smallest lattice. The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
| `worley.smallest.s1.f1` | `cloudgen.tiles.tileable_worley` | 0 | The smallest legal tile: one texel, one cell. F1 is the distance from the texel center (0.5, 0.5) to the jittered point, F2 to its image. The jitter grid the seed draws is the input plan0.jitter (freq, freq, 2: [row][col][x, y] offset inside the cell). Measured: every random input nudged by one float64 ulp (8 trials) and numpy.fft replaced by scipy.fft: max /diff/ 7.1e-15 over the seven worley cases (6.7e-13 relative on values above 1e-3), so 1e-9 relative / 1e-10 absolute; F1 and F2 are continuous in the jitter, no tie flips. |
